Only-begotten Son and Word of God, Who art immortal...

ANTIPHON THREE (Psalm 20 LXX)

In Thy strength the king shall be glad, O Lord,  
and in Thy salvation how greatly shall he exult!  
Thou hast given him his heart's desire,  
and hast not withheld the request of his lips.  
For Thou hast forestalled him with blessings of goodness;  
Thou hast set upon his head a crown of precious stone.  
[He asked life of Thee, and Thou gavest him length of days unto the ages of ages.]*  
    | Blessed art Thou,  
    | O Christ our  
    | God...  

---

* *Note of Violakis:* While this last verse is not indicated in the Typikon, it is found in the Pentekostarion, the Horologion, and the Imerologia of the Patriarchate.

The Entrance Hymn: **"Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will sing and praise Thy mighty acts! Save us, O Good Paraclete, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!"** (*Ὑψώθητι Κύριε ἐν τῇ δυνάμει σου, ᾄσομεν καὶ ψαλοῦμεν τὰς δυναστείας σου· Σῶσον ἡμᾶς, Παράκλητε ἀγαθέ, τοὺς ψάλλοντάς σοι, Ἀλληλούϊα*, Ps. 20:13 LXX). After the Entrance, the Dismissal Troparion: **"Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."** (*Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*), and the Kontakion in Tone 8: **"When the Most High came down and confused the tongues, He divided the nations; but when He distributed the tongues of fire, He called all to unity; and with one voice we glorify the All-holy Spirit!"** (*Ὅτε καταβὰς τὰς γλώσσας συνέχεε*). Instead of the Trisagion Hymn, we chant: **"As many as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia!"** (*Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε*). The Epistle (Acts 2:1–11) and the Gospel (John 7:37–52; 8:12) of the Feast. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Ninth Heirmos of the Iambic Canon in Tone 4: **"Without experiencing corruption didst thou conceive, and didst lend flesh unto the Word, the Artificer of all, O Mother unwedded, Virgin Theotokos, vessel of the Uncontainable, dwelling-place of thine Infinite Creator: thee do we magnify!"** (*Μὴ τῆς φθορᾶς διαπείρᾳ κυοφορήσασα*)...[^481]

# Pentecost Liturgy Concluded (§ 49) and Great Vespers of Pentecost with Kneeling Prayers (§ 50 Opening)

## Τῇ Κυριακῇ τῆς Πεντηκοστῆς (Τέλος Λειτουργίας) καὶ Ἑσπερινὸς τῆς Γονυκλισίας (§§ 49–50)

Τὸ πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθὸν ὁδηγήσει με ἐν γῇ εὐθείᾳ. 15. Εἰς τὸ Μετὰ φόβου, Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου καὶ ἡ ἐν τῷ Ἑσπερινῷ σημειωθεῖσα Ἀπόλυσις 16.

50.    Ἐν τῷ Μ. Ἑσπερινῷ, μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακόν, εἰς τὸ Κύριε ἐκέκραξα ψάλλομεν εἰς ς΄ τὰ γ΄ τῶν Αἴνων Ἰδιόμελα Παράδοξα σήμερον, Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε, Εἴσοδος μετὰ θυμιατοῦ, Φῶς ἱλαρόν 17, τὸ Μ. Προκείμενον μετὰ τῶν Στίχων αὐτοῦ ἅπαξ ἀπὸ τοῦ Βήματος καὶ ἀνὰ μίαν ἀπὸ {ἑκάστου τῶν δύο} Χορῶν, καὶ εὐθὺς ὁ Μ. Ἀρχιδιάκονος Ἔτι καὶ ἔτι κλίναντες τὰ γόνατα τοῦ Κυρίου δεηθῶμεν, καὶ τοῦ λαοῦ κλίναντος τὰ γόνατα καὶ ἐν εὐλαβείᾳ ἀκροωμένου ἀσκεποῦς, ὁ Πατριάρχης λέγει τὰς Εὐχὰς εἰς ἐπήκοον πάντων. Μετὰ τὴν β΄ Εὐχήν, Εὐλογητὸς εἶ, Κύριε Δέσποτα Παντοκράτορ, ὁ Διάκονος Ἀντιλαβοῦ, σῶσον, ἐλέησον, ἀνάστησον κτλ. καὶ ἡ Ἐκφώνησις Σὸν γάρ ἐστιν· ὁ Διάκονος τὸ Εἴπωμεν πάντες, ἡ Ἐκφώνησις Ὅτι ἐλεήμων καὶ φιλάνθρωπος, καὶ αὖθις ὁ Διάκονος Ἔτι καὶ ἔτι κλίναντες τὰ γόνατα, καὶ ἀναγινώσκονται αἱ ἕτεραι δύο Εὐχαί· εἰς τὸ τέλος ὁ Διάκονος Ἀντιλαβοῦ, σῶσον, ἐλέησον, ἀνάστησον κτλ. καὶ ἡ Ἐκφώνησις Εὐδοκίᾳ καὶ χάριτι τοῦ Μονογενοῦς σου Υἱοῦ· εἶτα τὸ Καταξίωσον Κύριε· μετὰ τοῦτο ὁ Διάκονος αὖθις Ἔτι καὶ ἔτι κλίναντες τὰ γόνατα, καὶ ἀναγινώσκονται καὶ αἱ τελευταῖαι τρεῖς Εὐχαί 18. Εἰς τὸ τέλος ὁ Διάκονος καὶ πάλιν Ἀντιλαβοῦ, σῶσον, ἐλέησον, ἀνάστησον κτλ. καὶ ἡ Ἐκφώνησις Σὺ γὰρ εἶ ἡ ἀνάπαυσις τῶν ψυχῶν καὶ τῶν σωμάτων ἡμῶν· ὁ Διάκονος Πληρώσωμεν τὴν ἑσπερινήν, ἡ Ἐκφώνησις Ὅτι

---

15. Τὸ ἀρχαῖον Τυπικόν, ὡς καὶ τὰ Πεντηκοστάρια, σημειοῖ διὰ τὴν σήμερον τοῦτο τὸ Κοινωνικὸν καὶ οὐχὶ Τὸ πνεῦμα σου τὸ ἅγιον μὴ ἀντανέλῃς ἀφ᾽ ἡμῶν, ὅπερ ἔθηκεν ὁ Κωνσταντῖνος, καίπερ ἐν τῇ Ἀποδόσει φαίνεται διορθῶν τὸ λάθος ὁρίζων Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθόν.
16. Ψαλλομένου τοῦ Κοινωνικοῦ, οἱ Διάκονοι λαμβάνουσι καιρόν, εἰσέρχονται εἰς τὸ Ἅγιον Βῆμα, καὶ ἐνδυθέντες τὰ ἱερὰ αὐτῶν ἄμφια, ἐξέρχονται μετὰ Δικηροτρικηρων καὶ ἵστανται περὶ τὸν θρόνον, εἰς δὲ τὴν Ἀπόλυσιν ὁ Πατριάρχης ἐνδύεται ἐπιτραχήλιον καὶ ὠμοφόριον καὶ ἀπὸ τοῦ θρόνου ἱστάμενος ποιεῖ Εὐλογητὸν καὶ ἄρχεται τοῦ Προοιμιακοῦ· οἱ δὲ Ἀρχιερεῖς λαβόντες τὸ Ἀντίδωρον, εἰσέρχονται εἰς τὸ Ἱερὸν Βῆμα.
17. Ἐπειδὴ ἕπεται Μέγα Προκείμενον, ψάλλεται ὁλόκληρον τὸ Φῶς ἱλαρὸν ἔξω· καὶ μόνον εἰς τὸ τέλος εἰσέρχεται ὁ Πατριάρχης εἰς τὸ Ἱερὸν καὶ ψάλλει ἐκεῖθεν τὸ Προκείμενον.
18. Ἐν τῇ τελευταίᾳ ἐκδόσει τοῦ Πεντηκοσταρίου τῇ κατὰ τὸ 1875 φέρονται τρεῖς αἱ Εὐχαὶ τῆς τελευταίας Στάσεως· ἀλλ᾽ ἐν τῷ ἀρχαίῳ Τυπικῷ τοῦ ͵αχπε΄ [1685] καὶ ἐν ἀρχαίαις ἐκδόσεσι τοῦ Πεντηκοσταρίου φέρονται αἱ τρεῖς αὗται Εὐχαὶ συνηνωμέναι εἰς δύο.

# Pentecost Liturgy Concluded (§ 49) and Great Vespers of Pentecost with Kneeling Prayers (§ 50 Opening)

## Pentecost Liturgy Concluded (§ 49) and Great Vespers of Pentecost with Kneeling Prayers (§ 50 Opening)

Communion Hymn: **"Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the land of uprightness. Alleluia!"** (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθὸν ὁδηγήσει με ἐν γῇ εὐθείᾳ*, Ps. 142:10 LXX) [Note 15]. At "With the fear of God...", we chant the Dismissal Troparion: **"Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."** (*Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*); then **"Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages"** (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and the Dismissal as indicated at Vespers [Note 16].

# THE SUNDAY OF PENTECOST: VESPERS
## (Great Vespers of Pentecost with the Kneeling Prayers)

50.    In Great Vespers, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant six verses of the three Idiomela Stichera of Matins: **"Marvelous things today have all the nations seen..."** (*Παράδοξα σήμερον*) (repeating each one once); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: **"O Heavenly King, Comforter..."** (*Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*). Entrance with the censer, **"O Joyful Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*) [Note 17]. The Great Prokimenon: Tone 7: **"Who is a god so great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders!"** (*Τίς Θεὸς μέγας ὡς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν; Σὺ εἶ ὁ Θεὸς ὁ ποιῶν θαυμάσια*, Ps. 76:13–14 LXX) with its three verses, chanted once from the Sanctuary [Bema] and once by each of the two Choirs; and immediately the Great Archdeacon intones: **"Again and again, bending our knees, let us pray to the Lord!"** (*Ἔτι καὶ ἔτι κλίναντες τὰ γόνατα τοῦ Κυρίου δεηθῶμεν*), and while the people bend their knees, listening in reverent devotion with heads uncovered, the Patriarch recites the Prayers to the hearing of all. After the second Prayer: **"Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Master Almighty..."**, the Deacon intones: **"Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up, O God, by Thy grace"**, and the Exclamation: **"For Thine it is to show mercy and to save us, O Lord our God, and unto Thee do we ascribe glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."** The Deacon intones: **"Let us all say with our whole soul and with our whole mind, let us say"**, with the Exclamation: **"For Thou art a merciful God and lovest mankind..."**. And immediately the Deacon intones: **"Again and again, bending our knees, let us pray to the Lord!"**, and the next two Prayers are read; at the conclusion, the Deacon intones: **"Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up, O God, by Thy grace"**, and the Exclamation: **"By the favor and grace of Thine only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."** Then the prayer **"Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin..."** (*Καταξίωσον Κύριε*). Immediately after this, the Deacon intones again: **"Again and again, bending our knees, let us pray to the Lord!"**, and the last three Prayers are read [Note 18]; at the conclusion, the Deacon intones once again: **"Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up, O God, by Thy grace"**, and the Exclamation: **"For Thou art the repose of our souls and bodies, and unto Thee do we ascribe glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."** Then the Deacon intones: **"Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord"**, and the Exclamation: **"For Thou art a good God and lovest mankind..."**. The Patriarch intones: **"Peace be to all"**... The Deacon: **"Let us bow our heads unto the Lord"**; the Patriarch reads the Prayer of Head-inclination and intones the Exclamation: **"May the might...**

---

*Note 15 (Violakis):* The ancient Typikon, as well as the Pentekostaria, note for today this Communion Hymn and not *"Take not Thy Holy Spirit from us,"* which Constantine [Protopsaltis] proposed and yet corrected in the Apodosis by prescribing *"Thy good Spirit."*  
*Note 16 (Violakis):* While the Communion Hymn is being sung, the Deacons take the time to enter the Holy Sanctuary and vest themselves; then they go forth with the Hierarchical Candlesticks (Dikerotrikera) and stand about the throne. At the Dismissal, the Patriarch vests with Epitrachilion and Omophorion and, standing upon the throne, intones *"Blessed is our God..."* and begins the Introductory Psalm; the Hierarchs, having received the Antidoron, enter the Holy Sanctuary.  
*Note 17 (Violakis):* Since a Great Prokimenon follows, *"O Joyful Light"* is chanted entirely outside the Sanctuary; and only at its conclusion does the Patriarch enter the Sanctuary and from thence chants the Prokimenon.  
*Note 18 (Violakis):* In the 1875 edition of the Pentekostarion, three Prayers are given for the final Stasis; but in the ancient Typikon of 1685 and in earlier editions of the Pentekostarion, these three Prayers appear united into two...[^482]

# Kneeling Vespers Concluded (§ 50), Monday of the Holy Spirit (§§ 51–52), and Apodosis Note

## Τῇ Κυριακῇ τῆς Πεντηκοστῆς (Τέλος Ἑσπερινοῦ), Τῇ Δευτέρᾳ τοῦ Ἁγίου Πνεύματος καὶ Ἀπόδοσις (§§ 50–52)

ἀγαθὸς καὶ φιλάνθρωπος, ὁ Πατριάρχης Εἰρήνη πᾶσι κτλ. Εἴη τὸ κράτος, καὶ ψάλλονται τὰ γ΄ Ἰδιόμελα Ἀπόστιχα Νῦν εἰς σημεῖον τοῖς πᾶσιν, Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, Δεῦτε λαοὶ τὴν Τρισυπόστατον θεότητα, Νῦν ἀπολύεις, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν ἐκ γ΄, καὶ ἡ Ἀπόλυσις παρὰ τοῦ Πατριάρχου Ὁ ἐκ τῶν πατρικῶν κόλπων κτλ. εἶτα τὸ Δι᾽ εὐχῶν.

51.    Τῇ Δευτέρᾳ τοῦ ἁγίου Πνεύματος, μετὰ τὸ Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεός, λέγεται τὸ Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεός, Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε, Τρισάγιον κτλ. ὁ Ν΄ ψαλμὸς τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον τῆς ἑορτῆς Ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς ὁ Θεὸς καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ τῆς Ἀπολύσεως τοῦ Μεσονυκτικοῦ· εἶτα μετὰ τὸ Εὐλογητός, τοῦ Ὄρθρου ὁ Ἑξάψαλμος ὡς σύνηθες. Εἰς τὸ Θεὸς Κύριος, τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον ἐκ γ΄ καὶ τὸ Ψαλτήριον, τὰ β΄ Καθίσματα τῆς α΄ καὶ β΄ Στιχολογίας ἐκ β΄, ὁ Ν΄ ψαλμὸς χῦμα καὶ οἱ Κανόνες ἀμφότεροι ὡς χθὲς ἄνευ Στίχων· ἀπὸ γ΄ Ὠδῆς, τὸ Κάθισμα Τὸ Πανάγιον Πνεῦμα ἅπαξ,, ἀφ᾽ ς΄ Κοντάκιον καὶ Οἶκος τῆς ἑορτῆς, τὸ Μηνολόγιον καὶ τὸ Ὑπόμνημα τῆς Δευτέρας τοῦ Ἁγίου Πνεύματος, αἱ Καταβασίαι Θείῳ καλυφθεὶς μόναι, εἶτα ἡ θ΄ Ὠδή, τὰ Ἐξαποστειλάρια τὸ α΄ δίς, καὶ τὸ ἕτερον ἅπαξ. Εἰς τοὺς Αἴνους, τὰ γ΄ Ἰδιόμελα Ἐν τοῖς Προφήταις ἀνήγγειλας εἰς δ΄. (Τὸ Πεντηκοστάριον ὑποδεικνύει ὅτι ἕκαστον τῶν 3 Ἰδιομέλων πρέπει νὰ ἐπαναλειφθῇ πρὸς τὸ σύνολον τῶν 6) Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, Γλῶσσαί ποτε συνεχύθησαν, Δοξολογία Μ. καὶ τὸ Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστέ.

Εἰς τὸ Ἐξαιρέτως Χαίροις ἄνασσα, Κοινωνικὸν Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἅγιον μὴ ἀντανέλῃς ἀφ᾽ ἡμῶν δεόμεθα, φιλάνθρωπε, τὸ Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς, Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου, καὶ Ἀπόλυσις Ὁ ἐν εἴδει πυρίνων γλωσσῶν . . .

# Kneeling Vespers Concluded (§ 50), Monday of the Holy Spirit (§§ 51–52), and Apodosis Note

## Kneeling Vespers Concluded (§ 50), Monday of the Holy Spirit (§§ 51–52), and Apodosis Note

...of Thy Kingdom be blessed and glorified, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." And then are chanted the three Idiomela Aposticha: Tone 3: **"Now as a sign unto all men, the Spirit of the Savior hath visibly shone forth upon the Apostles..."** (*Νῦν εἰς σημεῖον τοῖς πᾶσιν*); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: **"Come, all ye peoples, let us worship the Godhead in three Persons..."** (*Δεῦτε λαοὶ τὴν Τρισυπόστατον θεότητα*). # THE MONDAY OF THE HOLY SPIRIT
## (The Feast of the Holy Spirit)

51.    On Monday of the Holy Spirit, after **"Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."**, we say **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!"**, **"O Heavenly King, Comforter..."** (*Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*), the Trisagion, Psalm 50, the Dismissal Troparion of the Feast: **"Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."**, then **"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy..."**, and the rest of the Dismissal of the Midnight Office. Then, after the **"Blessed is our God..."** of Matins, the Six Psalms are recited as usual. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparion (three times), and the Psalter, the two Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis twice each, Psalm 50 recited plainly, and both Canons as yesterday without verses. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma: **"The All-holy Spirit, proceeding from the Father, and through the Son descended upon the unlettered Disciples..."** (*Τὸ Πανάγιον Πνεῦμα*) (once); after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: **"When the Most High came down..."**, the Ikos of the Feast, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Monday of the Holy Spirit. The Katavasias: **"Covered by a divine cloud, he that was slow of tongue proclaimed the Law written of God..."** (*Θείῳ καλυφθεὶς*) alone; then the 9th Ode is chanted. The first Exaposteilarion (twice) and the second (once). At the Praises: the three Idiomela: **"By the Prophets Thou didst proclaim unto us the way of salvation..."** (*Ἐν τοῖς Προφήταις ἀνήγγειλας*) to four [repeating the first] {Translator's Note: The Pentekostarion indicates that each of the three Idiomela must be repeated once for a total of six}; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: **"Once the tongues were confused on account of the audacity of the tower-building..."** (*Γλῶσσαί ποτε συνεχύθησαν*). The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal Troparion: **"Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."**.

52.    In the Divine Liturgy, the Antiphons and the Entrance Hymn of the Feast as yesterday; the Dismissal Troparion and the Kontakion. Instead of the Trisagion Hymn: **"As many as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia!"** (*Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε*). The Epistle (Ephesians 5:8–19) and the Gospel (Matthew 18:10–20) of the day. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Katavasia: **"Rejoice, O Queen, boasting of virgins and mothers!..."** (*Χαίροις Ἄνασσα*); Communion Hymn: **"Take not Thy Holy Spirit from us, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind. Alleluia!"** (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἅγιον μὴ ἀντανέλῃς ἀφ᾽ ἡμῶν δεόμεθα, φιλάνθρωπε*); **"We have seen the true Light..."** (*Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*), **"Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages"** (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and the Dismissal: **"May He Who sent down the All-holy Spirit from heaven in the form of fiery tongues upon His holy Disciples and Apostles, Christ our true God..."** (*Ὁ ἐν εἴδει πυρίνων γλωσσῶν...*).

    During this entire week of Holy Pentecost, fast-free privilege is observed; all foods are permitted.
    On Saturday of this week the Apodosis of the Feast of Pentecost takes place. The Service is sung exactly as prescribed in Paragraphs 47–49, with the exception, in Vespers [Note 19], of the Old Testament Readings; in Matins, of the Liti, of the Polyeleos with the Kathisma of the Third Stasis, and of the Matins Gospel. In the Divine Liturgy, we read the Epistle and Gospel of the day. At "Espe-...

---

*Note 19 (Violakis):* At "Lord, I have cried" we set for the Apodosis eight verses only...[^483]

# Apodosis Concluded (§ 52) and Sunday of All Saints (§§ 53–55 Opening)

## Ἡ Ἀπόδοσις τῆς Πεντηκοστῆς (Τέλος) καὶ Τῇ Κυριακῇ τῶν Ἁγίων Πάντων (§§ 52–55)

Ὄρθρου. Ἐν τῇ Λειτουργίᾳ Ἀπόστολος καὶ Εὐαγγέλιον λέγεται τῆς ἡμέρας. Εἰς τὸ Ἐξαιρέτως Χαίροις Ἄνασσα, Κοινωνικὸν Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθόν, Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς, Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου, Ἀπόλυσις καὶ Ἀπόδοσις.

    Μετὰ τὴν Ἀπόδοσιν τῆς ἑορτῆς, εἰς τὸ ἑξῆς Καταβασίαι ψάλλονται Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου, καὶ Κοντάκιον ἐν τῇ Λειτουργίᾳ Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν.

Τῌ ΚΥΡΙΑΚῌ ΤΩΝ ΑΓΙΩΝ ΠΑΝΤΩΝ

53.    Τῷ Σαββάτῳ ἑσπέρας, μετὰ τὸ Προοιμιακὸν καὶ τὸ Ψαλτήριον, εἰς τὸ Κύριε ἐκέκραξα ψάλλομεν Ἀναστάσιμα ς΄, καὶ τῶν Ἁγίων δ΄, Δόξα, Μαρτύρων θεῖος χορός, Καὶ νῦν, Ὁ βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν, Εἴσοδος, τὸ Προκείμενον καὶ τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα. Εἰς τὰ Ἀπόστιχα τὰ Ἀναστάσιμα Στιχηρά, Δόξα, Δεῦτε πιστοὶ σήμερον, Καὶ νῦν, Ὁ Ποιητὴς καὶ λυτρωτής μου, τὸ ἀναστάσιμον Ἀπολυτίκιον, τῶν Ἁγίων, τὸ Θεοτοκίον, καὶ Ἀπόλυσις.

54.    Τῇ Κυριακῇ εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον, μετὰ τὸν Ν΄ ψαλμόν, ὁ Τριαδικὸς κανών, ἡ Λιτὴ τῶν Ἁγίων, καὶ τὰ Ἄξιόν ἐστιν, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον τῶν Ἁγίων, καὶ ὁ Ἑξάψαλμος. Εἰς τὸ Θεὸς Κύριος τὰ Ἀπολυτίκια ὡς εἰς τὸ Ἑσπερινόν, τὸ Ψαλτήριον καὶ ὁ Ἄμωμος, τὰ ἀναστάσιμα Καθίσματα τῆς α΄ καὶ β΄ Στιχολογίας, τὰ Εὐλογητάρια, ἡ Ὑπακοὴ τοῦ ἤχου, οἱ Ἀναβαθμοί, καὶ τὸ Προκείμενον, ὁ Ἀναστάσιμος κανών, καὶ ὁ τῶν Ἁγίων. Ἀπὸ γ΄ Ὠδῆς τὸ ἀναστάσιμον Κοντάκιον μετὰ τοῦ Οἴκου χῦμα, εἶτα τὰ Κάθισμα τῶν Ἁγίων μετὰ τοῦ Θεοτοκίου· ἀφ᾽ ς΄ τὸ Κοντάκιον καὶ ὁ Οἶκος τοῦ Πεντηκοσταρίου, τὸ Μηνολόγιον, καὶ τὸ Ὑπόμνημα τῆς Κυριακῆς τῶν Ἁγίων Πάντων. Αἱ Καταβασίαι Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου, καὶ ἅπασα ἡ τάξις τοῦ Ἑωθινοῦ Εὐαγγελίου· εἶτα στιχολογοῦμεν Τὴν τιμιωτέραν, τὸ ἀναστάσιμον Ἐξαποστειλάριον, τὰ β΄ τῶν Ἁγίων καὶ τὸ Θεοτοκίον. Εἰς τοὺς Αἴνους, Ἀναστάσιμα δ΄, καὶ τῶν Ἁγίων δ΄. Δόξα, τὸ Ἑωθινὸν Εἰς τὸ Ὄρος τοῖς Μαθηταῖς, Καὶ νῦν, Ὑπερευλογημένη, Δοξολογία Μ. καὶ τὸ Σήμερον σωτηρία.

55.    Εἰς τὴν Λειτουργίαν, τὰ Τυπικὰ καὶ οἱ Μακαρισμοὶ τοῦ ἤχου εἰς δ΄, καὶ δ΄ ἐκ τῆς ς΄ Ὠδῆς τοῦ Κανόνος τῶν Ἁγίων Ὡς ἔντιμον, ἐκλεκτόν. Μετὰ τὴν Εἴσοδον, τὸ ἀναστάσιμον Ἀπολυτίκιον, τὸ

# Apodosis Concluded (§ 52) and Sunday of All Saints (§§ 53–55 Opening)

## Apodosis Concluded (§ 52) and Sunday of All Saints (§§ 53–55 Opening)

...cially, the Katavasia: **"Rejoice, O Queen, boasting of virgins and mothers!..."** (*Χαίροις Ἄνασσα*); Communion Hymn: **"Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the land of uprightness. Alleluia!"** (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθόν*). Then **"We have seen the true Light..."** (*Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*), **"Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages"** (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), Dismissal and the Apodosis.

    After the Apodosis of the Feast, the following Katavasias are sung henceforth: **"I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..."** (*Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*); and in the Divine Liturgy, we sing the Kontakion: **"O Protection of Christians unashamed, Mediatress before the Creator unwavering..."** (*Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*).

# THE FIRST SUNDAY AFTER PENTECOST: ALL SAINTS
## (The Sunday of All Saints)

53.    On Saturday evening, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Psalter (First Kathisma: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant six Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 8 and four of the Saints; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: **"The divine choir of the Martyrs, the foundation of the Church, the perfection of the Gospel..."** (*Μαρτύρων θεῖος χορός*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 6: **"Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?..."** (*Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε* / *Ὁ βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν*). Entrance with censer, **"O Joyful Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), the Prokimenon of the day: **"The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty!"** (*Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*, Ps. 92:1 LXX), and the Old Testament Readings (Isaiah 43:9–14; Wisdom of Solomon 3:1–9; 5:15–6:3 LXX). At the Aposticha: the Resurrectional Stichera; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: **"Come, all ye faithful today, let us celebrate in chorus with psalms and hymns..."** (*Δεῦτε πιστοὶ σήμερον*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: **"My Maker and Redeemer, Christ the Lord, proceeding from thy womb, O all-pure Virgin..."** (*Ὁ Ποιητὴς καὶ λυτρωτής μου*). The Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8, that of the Saints in Tone 4: **"Clothed in the blood of Thy Martyrs throughout all the world as in purple and fine linen, Thy Church through them crieth unto Thee, O Christ God: Send down Thy compassions upon Thy people; grant peace unto Thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy!"** (*Τῶν ἐν ὅλῳ τῷ κόσμῳ Μαρτύρων σου*); the Theotokion, and Dismissal.

54.    On Sunday at Matins, after Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon, the Liti of the Saints, and **"It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..."** (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*); then the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion of the Saints: **"Clothed in the blood of Thy Martyrs..."**, and the Six Psalms. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparia as in Vespers; then the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma). The Resurrectional Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis [Stichologia], the Resurrectional Evlogitaria (**"Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"**), the Ypakoe of Tone 8, the Anavathmoi of Tone 8, and the Prokimenon of Tone 8. The Canons: the Resurrectional Canon and that of the Saints. After the 3rd Ode, the Resurrectional Kontakion with its Ikos recited plainly, then the Kathisma of the Saints with its Theotokion; after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: **"Unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation, the world offereth the God-bearing Martyrs as first-fruits of nature..."** (*Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύσεως*), the Ikos of the Pentekostarion, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Sunday of All Saints. The Katavasias: **"I shall open my mouth..."** (*Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*), and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (First Eothinon: Matthew 28:16–20); then we chant with verses: **"More honorable than the Cherubim..."** (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν τῶν Χερουβίμ*). Exaposteilaria: the Resurrectional Exaposteilarion, the two of the Saints, and the Theotokion. At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera and four of the Saints; "Glory...": First Eothinon Doxastikon in Tone 1: **"When the Disciples were hastening to the mountain for the Ascension on high..."** (*Εἰς τὸ Ὄρος τοῖς Μαθηταῖς*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 2: **"Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..."** (*Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*). The Great Doxology, and **"Today is salvation come into the world..."** (*Σήμερον σωτηρία*).

55.    In the Divine Liturgy, the Typika and the four Beatitudes of Tone 8, and four hymns from the 6th Ode of the Canon of the Saints: **"As an honorable, chosen living temple..."** (*Ὡς ἔντιμον, ἐκλεκτόν*). After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, that of the Saints, that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion: **"Unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation..."**. The Epistle of the Saints (Hebrews 11:33–12:2) and Gospel of the Saints (Matthew 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30). At "Especially", **"It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..."** (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*)...[^484]

# All Saints Liturgy Concluded (§ 55)

## Τῇ Κυριακῇ τῶν Ἁγίων Πάντων (Τέλος Λειτουργίας) (§ 55)

τῶν Ἁγίων, τὸ τοῦ Ἁγίου τοῦ Ναοῦ, καὶ Κοντάκιον Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύσεως, Ἀπόστολος καὶ Εὐαγγέλιον τῶν Ἁγίων. Εἰς τὸ Ἐξαιρέτως· Ἄξιόν ἐστιν, Κοινωνικὸν Ἀγαλλιᾶσθε δίκαιοι ἐν Κυρίῳ, τοῖς εὐθέσι πρέπει αἴνεσις, Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς, Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου, καὶ Ἀπόλυσις.

# All Saints Liturgy Concluded (§ 55)

## All Saints Liturgy Concluded (§ 55)

...of the Saints, that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion in Tone 8: **"Unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation, the world offereth the God-bearing Martyrs as first-fruits of nature..."** (*Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύσεως*). The Epistle of the Saints (Hebrews 11:33–12:2) and Gospel of the Saints (Matthew 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30). At "Especially", **"It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..."** (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*); Communion Hymn: **"Rejoice in the Lord, O you righteous, praise is meet for the upright. Alleluia!"** (*Ἀγαλλιᾶσθε δίκαιοι ἐν Κυρίῳ, τοῖς εὐθέσι πρέπει αἴνεσις*, Ps. 32:1 LXX); **"We have seen the true Light..."** (*Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*), **"Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages"** (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal.[^485]

### Notes

[^458]: Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Holy Pascha (The Midnight Office Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p797 (printed page 782) and facing English leaf p798 (printed page 783). Major structural division: Ritual Order of the Services of the Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Pascha (Mesonyktikon / Midnight Office). At around the Fifth Hour of the night (11:00 p.m.), the clergy and faithful enter into the temple. The Priest intones the opening blessing: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (Eulogetos ho Theos); the Patriarch says: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King, Comforter, the Spirit of truth..." (Basileu ouranie); the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "Come, let us worship" (thrice), and Psalm 50 LXX plainly. The choirs chant the monumental Holy Saturday Canon in Tone 6: "Of old the surge of the sea covered the pursuing tyrant beneath the waves..." (Kymati thalasses) for six Troparia, each preceded by the refrain: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee"; after each Ode, the Heirmos is chanted slowly as Katavasia; the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] is omitted. At the Fourth Ode, the deacons, vested in their sacred vestments, exit from the Sanctuary bearing the two-branched and three-branched candlesticks [dikirotrikira]. The Patriarch, having venerated the holy icons and prepared himself according to custom for the Divine Liturgy, enters the Sanctuary and vests in all his hierarchical vestments, as do all the concelebrating Hierarchs. Following the Ninth Ode of the Canon, the Trisagion Prayers are recited from within the Sanctuary; then the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 2: "When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life Immortal, then didst Thou slay Hades with the brilliance of Thy Divinity..." (Hote katelthes pros ton thanaton); the Augmented Litany and the Dismissal of the Midnight Office are given. The Patriarch, preceded by the concelebrating Hierarchs, exits from the Sanctuary, chanting slowly with them the Sticheron in Tone 6: "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, the angels hymn in the heavens: enable us also on earth to glorify Thee with a pure heart" (Ten Anastasin sou Christe Soter), which is repeated also by the choirs until the procession reaches the appointed outdoor platform [dais / ambo]. Printed Footnote 1 of Violakis: Records the widespread local custom wherein the presiding Hierarch stands before the Beautiful Gate holding the two-branched and three-branched candlestick [dikirotrikiron] lit from the sleepless lamp upon the Holy Table, chanting: "Come, receive the light from the unwaning Light, and glorify Christ Who is risen from the dead!" (Deute labete phos ek tou anesperou photos), and summons the congregation to light their tapers. A translator's scholarly reference cites Alkiviadis C. Calivas's liturgical treatise *Great Week and Pascha in the Greek Orthodox Church* (pages 97–121) regarding the historical evolution of Holy Week and Paschal services.
[^459]: Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Holy Pascha (Paschal Matins: The Resurrection Service and Canon). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p799 (printed page 784) and facing English leaf p800 (printed page 785). Continuation of the Paschal Service at the outdoor platform: The Archdeacon exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel, let us pray unto the Lord God" (Kai hyper tou kataxiothenai hemas). The Patriarch proclaims the Holy Gospel: "In the end of the Sabbath, as it began to dawn toward the first day of the week..." (Opse sabbaton te epiphoskouse) [Matthew 28:1–20]. The choirs sing: "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla ete, Despota). Printed Footnote 2 of Violakis: Documents the liturgical practice in large cathedrals where this Gospel is proclaimed collaboratively by multiple Hierarchs and Priests, dividing the pericopes at verse 10 and continuing at verse 16 (omitting verses 11–15); it also notes the alternative tradition observed in various monasteries and parishes of proclaiming the Second Eothinon Gospel: "When the Sabbath was past" (Diagenomenou tou Sabbatou, Mark 16:1–8). The Patriarch takes the censer, censes the Holy Gospel Book three times, and intones the solemn Paschal Doxology: "Glory to the holy, and consubstantial, and life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (Doxa te hagia kai homoousio kai zoopoio kai adiaireto Triadi). The Patriarch sings the Paschal Troparion in Tone 5: "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!" (Christos aneste ek nekron) three times; the choirs repeat it six times, while the Patriarch censes and intones the four Paschal Verses of Psalm 67 LXX: 1. "Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered; let them also that hate Him flee before Him!" (Ps. 67:2 LXX); 2. "As smoke vanisheth, so let them vanish; as wax melteth before the fire!" (Ps. 67:3 LXX); 3. "So let the wicked perish at the presence of God; and let the righteous be glad!" (Ps. 67:3 LXX); 4. "This is the day which the Lord hath made; let us rejoice and be glad in it!" (Ps. 117:24 LXX), followed by "Glory... Both now and ever...". The Patriarch sings the Troparion once more while censing the clergy and people. The Archdeacon recites the Great Litany: "In peace let us pray unto the Lord" (En eirene tou Kyriou deetnomen), and the Patriarch sings the Exclamation: "For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (Hoti prepei soi pasa doxa). The clergy and people process back into the temple in solemn order. The Patriarch begins chanting the Paschal Canon of Saint John of Damascus in Tone 1: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant, O ye peoples!" (Anastaseos hemera) with the concelebrating Hierarchs, with six Troparia in each Ode preceded by the verse: "Glory to Thy holy Resurrection, O Lord!" (Doxa te hagia Anastasei sou, Kyrie); the final two Troparia are preceded by "Glory..." and "Both now and ever...". After each Ode, the Katavasia is chanted, followed by the Little Litany and the appointed Exclamations: Ode 1: "For Thine is the dominion..."; Ode 3: "For Thou art our God..."; Ode 4: "For Thou art a good God, and lovest mankind..."; Ode 5: "For sanctified and glorified is Thine all-honorable and majestic Name..."; Ode 6: "For Thou art the King of peace, and the Savior of our souls..."; Ode 7: "Blessed and glorified be the might of Thy kingdom..."; Ode 8: "For blessed is Thy Name, and glorified is Thy kingdom..."; Ode 9: "For all the powers of the heavens praise Thee...". After the 3rd Ode, the Ypakoe: "Anticipating the dawn, Mary and the women found the stone rolled away from the tomb..." (Prolabousai ton orthron) is read plainly. After the 6th Ode, the Paschal Kontakion, Ikos, and Synaxarion Memorial of the day are recited; then the Resurrectional hymn: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship the Holy Lord Jesus, the only sinless One..." (Anastasin Christou theasamenoi) is chanted three times (once by the Patriarch and twice by the concelebrating Hierarchs), followed by "Jesus having risen from the tomb, as He foretold, hath given us eternal life and great mercy" (Anastas ho Iesous apo tou taphou). Odes 7 and 8 follow; the Great Archdeacon exclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!", and the 9th Ode is chanted with the appointed Paschal Megalynaria.
[^460]: Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Holy Pascha (Paschal Matins Concluded: Ode 9 Megalynaria, Praises, and Liturgy Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p801 (printed page 786) and facing English leaf p802 (printed page 787). Concluding rubrics of Paschal Matins: At the Ninth Ode of the Paschal Canon of Saint John of Damascus in Tone 1, the appointed Megalynaria are chanted with their Troparia: 1. "Magnify, O my soul, Him Who suffered willingly, and was buried, and arose from the grave on the third day!" (Megalynon psyche mou ton ethelousios pathonta) with the Heirmos: "Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem, for the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee! Dance now and be glad, O Zion, and do thou, O pure Theotokos, rejoice in the Resurrection of Him Whom thou didst bear!" (Photizou, photizou he nea Ierousalem); 2. "Magnify, O my soul, Christ the Giver of life, Who arose from the grave on the third day!" (Megalynon psyche mou ton exanastanta trihemeron ek taphou Christon ton zoodoten) with the same Heirmos repeated; 3. "Christ is the new Pascha, the living sacrificial Victim, the Lamb of God Who taketh away the sin of the world!" (Christos to kainon Pascha) with the Troparion: "O Thy divine, O Thy beloved, O Thy sweetest voice!..." (O theias, o philes); 4. "Today the whole creation is glad and does rejoice, for Christ has risen, and Hades has been despoiled!" (Semeron pasa ktisis agalletai kai chairei) with the same Troparion repeated; "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit": "Magnify, O my soul, the dominion of the Undivided Godhead of Three Hypostases!" (Megalynon psyche mou, tes Trisypostatou kai adiairetou Theotetos to kratos) with the Troparion: "O great and most sacred Pascha, Christ! O Wisdom, and Word of God, and Power!..." (O Pascha to Mega kai hierotaton, Christe); "Both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen": "Rejoice, O Virgin, rejoice; rejoice, O blessed one; rejoice, O most glorified one, for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day!" (Chaire Parthene, chaire, chaire, eulogemene) with the same Troparion repeated. The Katavasia is chanted: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace: O pure Virgin, rejoice, and again I say rejoice; for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day. Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem..." (Ho Angelos eboa te Kecharitomene). The Little Litany [Petition] is recited, and the Exaposteilarion in Tone 2: "Having slept in the flesh as a mortal, O King and Lord, on the third day Thou didst rise again, raising up Adam from corruption and abolishing death: O Pascha of incorruption, salvation of the world!" (Sarki hypnosas hos thnetos) is sung three times. At the Praises, eight stichoi are set, chanting the four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 1 ("We hymn Thy saving Passion, O Christ...", "Thou Who didst endure the Cross...", "Thou Who didst despoil Hades...", "Glorifying Thy divine condescension...") and the four Stichera of Pascha in Tone 5 with their verses of Psalm 67 LXX ("Let God arise...", "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today...", "As smoke vanisheth...", "Come from that vision, O women...", "So let the wicked perish...", "The myrrh-bearing women...", "This is the day which the Lord hath made...", "A Pascha of delight! Pascha, the Lord's Pascha!..."). "Glory... Both now and ever...": Paschal Doxastikon in Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival, and let us embrace one another. Let us say, Brethren, even to those who hate us; let us forgive all things at the Resurrection, and thus let us cry:". At the words "and thus let us cry", the Second Choir sings the customary melody of "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!" (Christos aneste); then the First Choir chants the same, and again the Second Choir repeats it, and the Divine Liturgy begins. Printed Footnote 3 of Violakis: Documents that while this Doxastikon is chanted, the solemn veneration of the Holy Gospel Book takes place in all reverence and due order; in certain local traditions, however, the veneration is performed immediately at the beginning of the Paschal Canon for greater convenience of both the clergy and the congregation.
[^461]: Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Holy Pascha (The Divine Liturgy Opening) (§ 2 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p803 (printed page 788) and facing English leaf p804 (printed page 789). Paragraph 2: The Divine Liturgy of Holy Pascha: The Archdeacon exclaims: "Bless, Master!" (Eulogeson Despota); the Priest intones the Kingdom Doxology: "Blessed is the kingdom of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (Eulogemene he basileia), and the choirs respond: "Amen". Directly from within the Sanctuary, the celebrant chants the Paschal Troparion in Tone 5: "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!" (Christos aneste ek nekron) three times; the choirs then sing it six times with the Paschal Verses of Psalm 67 LXX ("Let God arise...", "As smoke vanisheth...", "So let the wicked perish...", "This is the day which the Lord hath made..."), and the final repetition is intoned again from within the Sanctuary. The Festal Antiphons of Pascha are chanted antiphonally with their appointed refrains: Antiphon 1: Psalm 65 LXX ("Shout joyfully unto the Lord, all the earth; chant now unto His Name, give glory unto His praise", "Say unto God: How awesome are Thy works! In the multitude of Thy power shall Thine enemies lie unto Thee", "Let all the earth worship Thee, and chant unto Thee; let them chant unto Thy Name, O Most High!") with the refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!" (Tais presbeiais tes Theotokou, Soter, soson hemas); Antiphon 2: Psalm 66 LXX ("May God be compassionate unto us, and bless us; may He cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us", "That we may know Thy way upon the earth, Thy salvation among all nations", "Let the peoples give thanks unto Thee, O God; let all the peoples give thanks unto Thee!") with the refrain: "Save us, O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead, who sing to Thee: Alleluia!" (Soson hemas Yie Theou, ho anastas ek nekron, psallontas soi, Allelouia); Antiphon 3: Psalm 67 LXX ("Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered...", "As smoke vanisheth, so let them vanish...", "So let the wicked perish at the presence of God; and let the righteous be glad!", "This is the day which the Lord hath made; let us rejoice and be glad in it!") with the refrain: "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!". At the Little Entrance, the clergy chant the Entrance Hymn [Isodikon]: "In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel!" (En ekklesiais eulogeite ton Theon, Kyrion ek pegon Israel, Ps. 67:27 LXX).
[^462]: Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Holy Pascha (The Divine Liturgy Concluded) (§ 2 Concluded) and Paschal Vespers Opening (§ 3 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p805 (printed page 790) and facing English leaf p806 (printed page 791). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 2 (The Divine Liturgy of Pascha): The Entrance Hymn is completed with the Paschal refrain: "Save us, O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead, who sing to Thee: Alleluia!". Directly after the Entrance: the Troparion "Christ is risen from the dead...", the Ypakoe in Tone 4: "Anticipating the dawn, Mary and the women found the stone rolled away from the tomb..." (Prolabousai ton orthron), and the Kontakion in Tone 8: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One, yet didst Thou destroy the power of Hades..." (Ei kai en tapho katelthes). In place of the Trisagion Hymn, the Baptismal Hymn: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia" (Hosoi eis Christon ebaptisthete, Gal. 3:27) is chanted. The Epistle is proclaimed: Acts 1:1–8 (The Acts of the Apostles: The Promise of the Father and the Ascension); the "Alleluia" with its verses; and the Holy Gospel: John 1:1–17 (The Paschal Prologue: "In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God..."). In place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk], the choirs chant the Ninth Heirmos with its Megalynarion: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace: O pure Virgin, rejoice, and again I say rejoice; for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day. Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem, for the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee! Dance now and be glad, O Zion, and do thou, O pure Theotokos, rejoice in the Resurrection of Him Whom thou didst bear!" (Ho Angelos eboa... Photizou, photizou). The Communion Hymn is chanted: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!" (Soma Christou metalabete, peges athanatou geusasthe. Allelouia). After the Prayer behind the Ambo, the Troparion "Christ is risen from the dead..." is intoned three times plainly, and the Patriarch reads from the ambo the monumental Catechetical Sermon of Saint John Chrysostom: "If any be pious and a lover of God, let him enjoy this fair and radiant triumph! If any be a wise servant, let him rejoicing enter into the joy of his Lord!..." (Ei tis eusebes kai philotheos). At its conclusion, the Dismissal Troparion of Saint John Chrysostom in Tone 8 is chanted: "Grace shining forth from thy mouth like a radiant torch has enlightened the universe; it has disclosed to the world the treasures of poverty, and demonstrated to us the height of humility; but as thou dost instruct us by thy words, Father John Chrysostom, intercede with Christ God that our souls may be saved!" (He tou stomatos sou kathaper pyrsos eklampsasa charis); the blessing of the Lord is given, and the solemn Dismissal is pronounced by the Patriarch, who exclaims three times: "Christ is risen!", the people responding thrice: "Truly He is risen!". The Patriarch exclaims: "Glory to His holy third-day Resurrection!", the people responding: "We worship His third-day Resurrection!", and the Patriarch concludes by singing the entire Troparion "Christ is risen from the dead...". Following the Dismissal, the Patriarch and concelebrating Hierarchs in full vestments, preceded by taper-bearers, processional fans [hexapteryga], and church officers, ascend to the Great Synodikon while the choirs chant "Christ is risen from the dead...", where the Polychronismos of the Patriarch is chanted according to custom. Paragraph 3 Opening: The Vespers of Pascha [Agape Vespers]: Around the Third Hour of the day [9:00 a.m.], the Patriarch and Hierarchs assemble in the Great Synodikon and vest in their hierarchical vestments, attended by priests and deacons already vested. Printed Footnotes 4–8 of Violakis: Footnote 4: The Entrance Hymn "In the congregations" is chanted daily at the Divine Liturgy until the Apodosis of Pascha; Footnote 5: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ" replaces the Trisagion until Saturday of Bright Week and on the Apodosis; Footnote 6: Chanting "Christ is risen" three times after the Prayer behind the Ambo continues until Saturday of Bright Week, whereas from Thomas Sunday onward "Blessed be the name of the Lord" is sung; Footnote 7: In ancient Typika, Chrysostom's Catechetical Sermon was read after the Praises before the Divine Liturgy as preparation for Holy Communion, but later practice permanently fixed it after the Communion Hymn or Prayer behind the Ambo; Footnote 8: From Holy Pascha until Saturday of Bright Week, and on the Apodosis, the entire liturgical cycle of the Hours (First, Third, Sixth, Ninth Hours), Compline, and Midnight Office is replaced by the Paschal Service: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", "Christ is risen from the dead" (thrice), "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (thrice), the Ypakoe "Anticipating the dawn...", the Kontakion "Though Thou didst descend into the grave...", the Troparia "In the tomb bodily...", "Glory...", "As life-bearing...", "Both now and ever...", "That which is sanctified by the Most High...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "More honorable than the Cherubim", "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...", and "Christ is risen from the dead" (thrice). This service is repeated three times daily in place of the regular offices.
[^463]: Pentekostarion: The Sunday of Holy Pascha (Paschal Agape Vespers Concluded) (§ 3 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p807 (printed page 792) and facing English leaf p808 (printed page 793). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 3 (The Vespers of Love / Agape Vespers): While the Patriarch and concelebrating Hierarchs vest in the Great Synodikon, the choirs chant the slow Paschal Heirmos in Tone 1: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant, O ye peoples!" (Anastaseos hemera argon). A solemn procession forms: led by candle-bearers, processional fans [hexapteryga], the Primikirios, and the choirs chanting "Christ is risen from the dead...", followed by priests, Hierarchs, deacons holding the two-branched and three-branched candlesticks [dikirotrikira], and finally the Patriarch bearing his pastoral staff and the Holy Gospel Book, escorted by the Grand Logothete, ambassadors of Orthodox nations, and civil dignitaries. Processing down into the church, the Patriarch and clergy enter directly into the Sanctuary. The Patriarch censes three times before the Holy Table and intones the opening doxology: "Glory to the holy, and consubstantial, and life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", singing "Christ is risen from the dead..." according to the order of Paschal Matins. The Great Litany is recited by the Archdeacon, and the Patriarch intones the exclamation: "For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship...". At "Lord, I have cried" (Kyrie ekekraxa), six Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 2 are chanted in order: "Before the ages, begotten of the Father, God the Word incarnate of the Virgin Mary, come, let us worship; for having endured the Cross, He was delivered over to burial, as He Himself willed, and having risen from the dead, He saved me, the wandering man!" (Ton pro aionon ek Patros gennethenta), while the Patriarch, preceded by the Primikirios bearing the two-branched candle [divamvoulon], censes the icons and congregation, and ascends the throne. The First Choir sings "Glory...": "The saving hymn we sing with our mouths: Come, let us all worship in the house of the Lord..." (Ton soterion hymnon); the Second Choir sings "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 2: "The shadow of the law passed away when grace arrived..." (Parelthen he skia tou nomou). The Little Entrance is made with the Holy Gospel Book; "O Gladsome Light" (Phos hilaron) is chanted slowly and entirely outside the Sanctuary. The Patriarch enters the Sanctuary singing the Great Prokimenon in Tone 7: "What God is as great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders!" (Tis Theos megas hos ho Theos hemon; sy ei ho Theos ho poion thaumasia, Ps. 76:14 LXX) with its three verses (Ps. 76:15, 11, 12 LXX), repeated antiphonally by the choirs. The Patriarch exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel, let us pray unto the Lord God", and proclaims the Holy Gospel: John 20:19–25 ("When it was evening on that day, the first day of the week..." until "Except I shall see in His hands the print of the nails... I will not believe"), proclaimed in successive pericopes and translated/echoed in multiple languages by the concelebrating Hierarchs, priests, and deacons. After the choirs chant "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla ete), the Augmented Litany, "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin", and the Evening Litany are recited. After the exclamation: "Blessed and glorified be the might of Thy kingdom: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", the First Choir chants the Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 2: "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, hath enlightened the whole universe and recalled Thine own creation: O almighty Lord, glory to Thee!" (He Anastasis sou Christe Soter), followed by the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (Pascha hieron), "Glory... Both now and ever...": "It is the day of Resurrection...", and "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times). During this chanting, the Patriarch and clergy process back up to the Great Synodikon, where the Patriarch gives the solemn Dismissal while the choirs chant his Polychronismos; thus concluding the Agape Vespers.
[^464]: Bright Week: Bright Monday (§§ 4–5) and Bright Friday: The Life-Giving Fountain Opening (§ 6 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p809 (printed page 794) and facing English leaf p810 (printed page 795). Paragraph 4: Monday of Bright Week Matins: Following the Paschal replacement service for the Midnight Office (Paragraph 2, Note 8), the Priest, fully vested in all priestly vestments, stands before the Holy Table with the censer, censes, and intones: "Glory to the holy, and consubstantial, and life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."; he then chants "Christ is risen from the dead..." three times with the Paschal Verses. The Litany is recited with the exclamation: "For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship...". The Paschal Canon of Saint John of Damascus in Tone 1 is chanted without variation, except that after Ode 6 only the Kontakion ("Though Thou didst descend into the grave...") is said, followed directly by "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." (once). After Ode 8, the Deacon intones: "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!", and Ode 9 is sung with the Megalynaria. The Exaposteilarion: "Having slept in the flesh as a mortal..." is sung twice according to ancient typika. At the Praises, four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 2 ("Let every breath and all creation glorify Thee, O Lord...") and the Stichera of Pascha ("A sacred Pascha...") are chanted with their verses; "Glory... Both now and ever...": "It is the day of Resurrection...", "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times), and the Divine Liturgy begins. Paragraph 5: Monday of Bright Week Divine Liturgy: The Antiphons are celebrated as on Pascha Sunday. In place of the Trisagion, "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia" (Gal. 3:27) is sung; the Epistle (Acts 1:12–17, 21–26) and Holy Gospel (Luke 24:12–35: The Road to Emmaus) of the day are proclaimed. In place of "It is truly right", the Ninth Heirmos with Megalynarion: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace... Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem..." is chanted. Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!". After the Prayer behind the Ambo, "Christ is risen from the dead..." is intoned three times plainly, followed by the Dismissal. General Rubric for Bright Week: This identical order of service is to be strictly observed on each day throughout the entirety of Bright Week at Vespers, Matins, and the Divine Liturgy, with the sole exception of the Gospel reading proclaimed at the Vespers of Pascha Sunday afternoon. Paragraph 6: Friday of Bright Week: The Feast of the Most Holy Lady Theotokos of the Life-Giving Fountain: On Thursday evening at "Lord, I have cried" (Kyrie ekekraxa), ten Stichera are chanted: six Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 3: "Holding the victory, O Christ, over Hades, Thou didst ascend upon the Cross, that Thou mightest raise up with Thyself those who sat in the darkness of death: O Free among the dead, Who pourest forth life from Thine own light, almighty Savior, have mercy on us!" (Niken echon Christe) and four of the Life-Giving Fountain in Tone 3, repeating the first: 1. & 2. "Who can declare thy mighty wonders, O Fountain? For flowing with streams of wonders, thou dost pour forth rivers of healings..." (Tis lalesei tas dynasteias sou Pege). "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Fountain in Tone 6: "Who can tell of thy mighty acts, O undefiled Fountain? For abundant in miracles and lavish in grace, thou dost ever pour forth healings..." (Tis lalesei tas dynasteias sou Pege); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 3: "Who would not admire, who would not praise thy childbirth without seed, O all-pure Virgin? For the Son Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the Only-Begotten One, came forth from thee, pure Maiden, ineffably incarnate: God by nature, yet for our sakes becoming man by nature..." (Tis me makarisei se, Panagia Parthene). The Little Entrance is made with the censer; "O Gladsome Light" (Phos hilaron) is chanted, followed by the Great Prokimenon in Tone 7: "I will love Thee, O Lord, my strength: the Lord is my foundation, my refuge, and my deliverer!" (Agapeso se, Kyrie, he ischys mou, Ps. 17:2–3 LXX) with its appointed verses. At the Aposticha, begun by the Second Choir, the Resurrectional Sticheron: "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior..." (Ten Anastasin sou Christe Soter) is chanted.
[^465]: Bright Week: Friday of Bright Week (Feast of the Life-Giving Fountain Concluded) (§§ 6–8 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p811 (printed page 796) and facing English leaf p812 (printed page 797). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 6: At the Aposticha of Thursday evening Vespers, the Second Choir begins the Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 3: "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, the angels hymn in heaven...", followed by the three Prosomia of the Theotokos; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "Let us sound the trumpet of hymns, O ye lovers of the feast, let us leap for joy in songs, and let us dance exultantly in the ever-flowing Fountain of the Queen of all!..." (*Σαλπίσωμεν φιλέορτοι ἐν ὕμνοις*); "Both now and ever...": Paschal Doxastikon in Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival...", "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times), and Dismissal. Printed Translator's Note 1: Notes later liturgical practice in archdiocesan and patriarchal Imerologia (HABNA 1985, p. 85; 1990, p. 105; 1992, p. 106; IEP 1996, p. 1196; 1999, p. 298), directing the order: "Christ is risen...", "Thy temple, O Theotokos...", and "Christ is risen...". Paragraph 7: Friday of Bright Week Matins: The Paschal Canon of Saint John of Damascus in Tone 1 and the Canon of the Theotokos in Tone 3 are chanted with four Troparia for each ode; for the Canon of the Theotokos the verse is "Most Holy Theotokos, save us!"; at the conclusion of each ode, the Katavasia and "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times) as in § 4. After Ode 3: Paschal Kontakion recited plainly, and the Kathisma: "The ever-flowing and living fountain..." (*Τὴν ἀέναον κρήνην καὶ ζωηρὰν*) once. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Theotokos, Menologion of the day, verses and Synaxarion Hypomnema of the Life-Giving Fountain, "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." (three times), followed by Odes 7 and 8. Then Ode 9 with the appointed Megalynaria, and for the Canon of the Theotokos the verse "Most Holy Theotokos, save us!"; Exaposteilarion of Pascha and that of the Theotokos. At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera, three of the Theotokos, and the first Sticheron of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*). Printed Translator's Note 2: Notes that while Violakis sets 8 Stichera, later practice in the Pentekostarion and Patriarchal Imerologia sets 12 Stichera (4 Resurrectional, 4 of the Theotokos, and all 4 Paschal Stichera). "Glory... Both now and ever...": "It is the day of Resurrection...", "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times), and the Divine Liturgy begins. Paragraph 8 Opening: Friday of Bright Week Divine Liturgy: Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn. After the Entrance: "Christ is risen...", the Ypakoe "Anticipating the dawn...", Troparion of the Theotokos: "From Thy never-failing spring, O woman full of the grace of God..." (*Ἐξ ἀκενώτου σου πηγῆς θεοχαρίτωτε*), and Kontakion "Though Thou didst descend into the grave...". Printed Translator's Note 3: Notes that "From Thy never-failing spring" is structurally the Kontakion of the Feast, whereas the canonical Dismissal Troparion of the Theotokos is "Thy Temple, O Theotokos" (*Ὁ Ναός σου, Θεοτόκε*). In place of the Trisagion Hymn: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia" (Gal. 3:27). Epistle (Acts 3:1–8) and Gospel (John 2:12–22) of the day. In place of "It is truly right": Ninth Heirmos with Megalynarion: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace... Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem...".
[^466]: Bright Friday Concluded (§ 8 Concluded) and The Sunday of Antipascha / Thomas Sunday Opening (§§ 9–10). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p813 (printed page 798) and facing English leaf p814 (printed page 799). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 8 (Friday of Bright Week Divine Liturgy): At the Diptychs, the Megalynarion of the Feast is chanted: "Water of the life-flowing Fountain..." (*Ὕδωρ τὸ ζωῤῥύτον τῆς Πηγῆς*). Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!" (*Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*), and "Christ is risen from the dead...". After the Prayer behind the Ambo: "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times plainly), and Dismissal. Paragraph 9: The Sunday of Antipascha (Thomas Sunday) Vespers: On Saturday evening, the Ninth Hour of Pascha is recited according to the Paschal replacement order (§ 2, Note 8). After "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", "Christ is risen from the dead..." is sung once from the Sanctuary and once by each choir; immediately the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) is recited and the First Kathisma of the Psalter (First Stasis: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX). At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), six Idiomelon Stichera of the Feast are chanted in order; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "When the doors were closed..." (*Τῶν θυρῶν κεκλεισμένων*); Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", and Saturday Evening Great Prokimenon: "The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty!" (*Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*, Ps. 92:1 LXX) with its verses. At the Aposticha: three Idiomela; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "O Lover of mankind, great and unsearchable is the multitude of Thy compassions!..." (*Φιλάνθρωπε μέγα καὶ ἀνείκαστον*); Dismissal Troparion in Tone 7: "While the tomb was sealed, Thou, O Life, didst shine forth from the grave, O Christ God..." (*Ἐσφραγισμένου τοῦ μνήματος*) three times, and Dismissal. Paragraph 10: Sunday of Antipascha Matins: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon in Tone 1 is chanted, then the Liti of the Feast, and the Triadic hymn: "It is truly right to glorify Thee, God the Word..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*), Trisagion, Dismissal Troparion, and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord": Dismissal Troparion "While the tomb was sealed" (three times); the Psalter and the Polyeleos (Pss. 134–135 LXX); three Kathismata chanted twice each (the Resurrectional Evlogitaria are omitted today because it is a Master Feast of the Lord, as noted in the ancient Typika); immediately the Anavathmoi of Tone 4 (First Antiphon: "From my youth many passions fight against me..."); Prokimenon in Tone 4: "Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Zion!" (*Ἐπαίνει Ἱερουσαλὴμ τὸν Κύριον*, Ps. 147:1 LXX). Printed Footnote 9 of Violakis: Documents that on the Sunday of Antipascha, the Matins Gospel is proclaimed after the Katavasias, as on all other Sundays throughout the year, and not immediately following the Prokimenon. Canon of the Feast to six without verses. After Ode 3: Ypakoe in Tone 6: "As Thou didst stand in the midst of Thy disciples, O Savior, giving them peace: come also unto us and save us!" (*Ὡς ἐν μέσῳ τῶν Μαθητῶν σου*); after Ode 6: Kontakion in Tone 8: "With his inquisitive right hand, Thomas explored Thy life-giving side, O Christ God..." (*Τῇ φιλοπράγμονι δεξιᾷ*), the Ikos, Menologion of the day, and Hypomnema of the Feast; Katavasias of Pascha ("It is the day of Resurrection..."), and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (First Eothinon: Matthew 28:16–20); then Ode 9 of the Canon, Katavasia "The Angel cried... Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem..."; "Holy is the Lord our God" twice, and the verse: "Exalt ye the Lord our God, and worship at His footstool, for He is holy!" (Ps. 98:5 LXX). Printed Footnote 10 of Violakis: Notes that the ancient Typikon records only the Exaposteilaria without mention of "Holy is the Lord our God", likely omitted as this is a Feast of the Lord. Exaposteilarion of the Feast: "With my hand have I explored Thy side..." (*Ἐμῇ χειρὶ ἠρεύνησα*), the first twice and the second once. At the Praises: three Prosomia to four; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: "Eight days after Thy Resurrection, O Jesus our King..." (*Μεθ᾿ ἡμέρας ὀκτώ*); Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion.
[^467]: Sunday of Antipascha Divine Liturgy and Vespers (§§ 11–12), and The Apodosis of the Feast of Thomas (§§ 13–14 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p815 (printed page 800) and facing English leaf p816 (printed page 801). Paragraph 11: Sunday of Antipascha Divine Liturgy: Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn. After the Entrance: Dismissal Troparion "While the tomb was sealed", and Kontakion "Though Thou didst descend into the grave...". Trisagion Hymn, Epistle (Acts 5:12–20) and Gospel (John 20:19–31) of the Feast. In place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Ninth Heirmos of the Feast: "O Thou radiant lamp and Mother of God, unsetting torch of the divine Glory, thee do we magnify in hymns!" (*Σὲ τὴν φαεινὴν λαμπάδα*). Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Zion! Alleluia!" (*Ἐπαίνει Ἱερουσαλὴμ τὸν Κύριον*, Ps. 147:1 LXX), and "Christ is risen from the dead...". After the Prayer behind the Ambo: "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (three times), and Dismissal. Paragraph 12: Sunday of Antipascha Vespers: On Sunday evening, at "Lord, I have cried", three Stichera from the Praises: "After Thine awesome Resurrection from the tomb..." (*Μετὰ τὴν ἐκ τάφου σου φρικτήν*), and three of the Saint of the day from the Menaion; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 1: "When the doors were closed..." (*Τῶν θυρῶν κεκλεισμένων*); Entrance with censer, "O Gladsome Light", and Great Prokimenon: "What God is as great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders!" (*Τίς Θεὸς Μέγας*, Ps. 76:14 LXX) with its verses. At the Aposticha: Resurrectional Sticheron: "Our evening prayers receive, O holy Lord..." (*Τὰς ἑσπερινὰς ἡμῶν εὐχάς*), and two Penitential Stichera; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 1: "Eight days after..." (*Μεθ᾿ ἡμέρας ὀκτώ*); Dismissal Troparion "While the tomb was sealed" (once), and Dismissal. General rubric for Monday morning: The service is chanted as prescribed in the Pentekostarion. Paragraph 13: Saturday of Thomas Week: The Apodosis of the Feast of Thomas Vespers: On Friday evening, at "Lord, I have cried", the first three Stichera of the Feast: "When the doors were closed...", and three from the Menaion; "Glory...": "When the doors were closed..."; "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 1: "The Joy of all the world, who sprang forth from mankind and gave birth unto the Master..." (*Τὴν παγκόσμιον δόξαν*); Prokimenon of the day. At the Aposticha: four Resurrectional Stichera; "Glory... Both now and ever...": "O Lover of mankind, great and unsearchable..."; Dismissal Troparion "While the tomb was sealed" (three times), and Dismissal. Paragraph 14 Opening: Saturday of Thomas Week: Matins of the Apodosis: After the Midnight Office, Trisagion, Dismissal Troparion, and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord": Dismissal Troparion "While the tomb was sealed" (three times); Psalter; Kathismata: at the First Stasis of the Kathisma, the three Resurrectional Kathismata in Tone 1: "Thy tomb, O Savior, the soldiers guarding..." (*Τὸν τάφον σου Σωτήρ*), etc.; at the Second Stasis: "For fear of the Jews was Thy tomb sealed, O Savior..." (*Τῷ φόβῳ τῶν Ἑβραίων*) twice; "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." and Psalm 50 recited plainly; Canon of the Feast with its verses, and Canon of the Menaion. After Ode 3: Kathisma of the Menaion and of the Feast; after Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, and Menologion of the Saints of the day; Katavasias of Pascha; Ode 9 of the Feast; Katavasia "The Angel cried... Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem..."; the two Exaposteilaria once each. At the Praises: three Resurrectional Stichera...
[^468]: Saturday of Thomas Week Concluded (§§ 14–15) and The Third Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women (§§ 16–17 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p817 (printed page 802) and facing English leaf p818 (printed page 803). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 14 (Matins of the Apodosis): At the Praises, three Resurrectional Stichera from the Octoechos in Tone 1: "We hymn Thy saving Passion, O Christ, and glorify Thy Resurrection!" (*Ὑμνοῦμέν σου Χριστέ*), and three of the Feast: "After Thine awesome Resurrection from the tomb..."; "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Eight days after..."; Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion of the Feast. Paragraph 15: Divine Liturgy of the Apodosis: Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn. (*Τῇ φιλοπράγμονι δεξιᾷ*). Epistle (Acts 5:21–33) and Gospel (John 3:22–33) of the day. Alleluia!", "Christ is risen from the dead...", "Blessed be the name of the Lord", and Dismissal. Paragraph 16: The Third Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women Vespers: On Saturday evening, after the Introductory Psalm and the Psalter (First Kathisma: "Blessed is the man"): at "Lord, I have cried", seven Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 2, and three of the Myrrh-bearers; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "The Myrrh-bearing Women, reaching Thy tomb at deep dawn..." (*Αἱ Μυροφόροι Γυναῖκες*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 2: "The shadow of the law passed away when grace arrived..." (*Παρῆλθεν ἡ σκιὰ τοῦ νόμου*). At the Aposticha: Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 2: "Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, hath enlightened the whole universe..." (*Ἡ Ἀνάστασίς σου Χριστὲ Σωτήρ*), and the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." with their verses; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Paschal Doxastikon in Tone 5: "Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment..." (*Σὲ τὸν ἀναβαλλόμενον τὸ φῶς*); the three Dismissal Troparia of the Myrrh-bearers: 1. Tone 2: "The noble Joseph, taking down Thine immaculate Body from the Tree..." (*Ὁ εὐσχήμων Ἰωσήφ*), 2. "Glory...": "When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life Immortal, then didst Thou slay Hades with the brilliance of Thy Godhead..." (*Ὅτε κατῆλθες πρὸς τὸν θάνατον*), 3. "Both now and ever...": "Unto the myrrh-bearing women, the Angel standing by the tomb cried aloud: Myrrh is fitting for the dead, but Christ is shown to be a stranger to corruption!..." (*Ταῖς μυροφόροις γυναιξί*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 17 Opening: Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women Matins: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon, the Liti of the Myrrh-bearers, and the Triadic "It is truly right", Trisagion, Dismissal Troparion "The noble Joseph", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord": the three Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers; the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma); Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis; Resurrectional Evlogitaria: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"; Ypakoe of Tone 2; Anavathmoi of Tone 2; Prokimenon in Tone 2: "Arise, O Lord my God, in the precept which Thou hast commanded..." (Ps. 7:7 LXX); Canon of Pascha with the two Theotokia of Saint John of Damascus. Printed Footnote 11 of Violakis: Documents that the Canon of the Myrrh-bearers, being read daily throughout the ensuing week, is customarily omitted on this Sunday; and the exact same omission applies to the succeeding Sundays: of the Paralytic, of the Samaritan Woman, and of the Blind Man. After Ode 3: the two Kathismata; after Ode 6: Kontakion in Tone 2: "Thou didst command the myrrh-bearing women to rejoice, O Christ God..." and the Ikos, Menologion of the day, and Hypomnema of the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers; Katavasias of Pascha, and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (Second Eothinon: Mark 16:1–8); then Ode 9 of Pascha with the Megalynaria and the two Theotokia; Exaposteilarion of Pascha: "Having slept in the flesh as a mortal..." and that of the Myrrh-bearers: "Hearken, ye women, to the voice of joy...". At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 2, and the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha..."; "Glory...": Second Eothinon Doxastikon in Tone 2: "Coming with myrrhs, Mary's companions were perplexed..." (*Μετὰ μύρων προσελθούσαις*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection..."; Great Doxology...
[^469]: Sunday of the Myrrh-Bearing Women Concluded (§§ 17–19) and The Fourth Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Paralytic (§§ 20–21 Opening). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 17 (Matins of the Myrrh-bearers): At the conclusion of the Great Doxology, the Dismissal Troparion: "Today is salvation come into the world: let us sing unto Him Who arose from the tomb, the Author of our life!..." Paragraph 20: The Fourth Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Paralytic Vespers: On Saturday evening, after the Introductory Psalm and the Psalter (First Kathisma): at "Lord, I have cried", seven Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 3, and three of the Paralytic repeating the first; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 5: "Jesus went up to Jerusalem to the Sheep's Pool..." (*Ἀνέβη ὁ Ἰησοῦς εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 3: "How can we not marvel at thy childbirth, wherein God and man are united?..." (*Πῶς μὴ θαυμάσωμεν*). At the Aposticha: Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 3: "Thou Who by Thy Passion, O Christ, didst darken the sun, and by the light of Thy Resurrection didst enlighten all things: accept our evening hymn, O Lover of mankind!" (*Ὁ τῷ πάθει σου Χριστέ*), and the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha..."; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "In Solomon's porch lay a multitude of the sick..." (*Ἐν τῇ στοᾷ τοῦ Σολομῶντος*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection..."; Dismissal Troparion in Tone 3: "Let the heavens rejoice, let earthly things be glad!..." (*Εὐφραινέσθω τὰ Οὐράνια*); Theotokion in Tone 3: "Thou Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure the Cross, O Good One..." (*Σὲ τὴν μεσιτεύσασαν*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 21 Opening: Sunday of the Paralytic Matins: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon in Tone 3; at the Liti, "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 5: "At the Sheep's Pool a man lay sick for thirty and eight years..." (*Ἐπὶ τῇ προβατικῇ κολυμβήθρᾳ*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "A temple and gate art thou, a palace and throne of the King, O all-honored Virgin..." (*Ναὸς καὶ πύλη*); and the Triadic "It is truly right", Trisagion, Dismissal Troparion, and the Six Psalms. Chant unto our King, chant!" (Ps. 46:7 LXX); Canon of Pascha with the two Theotokia of Saint John of Damascus.
