(*Ῥῆμα Παράλυτον*); "Both now and ever...": Kathisma in Tone 4: "Awed by the beauty of thy virginity..." (*Τὴν ὡραιότητα*). After Ode 6: Kontakion in Tone 3: "As of old Thou didst raise up the Paralytic, O Lord, by Thy divine presence: so raise up my soul, paralyzed by grievous sins and shameful deeds!..." and Ikos, Menologion of the day, and Hypomnema of the Sunday of the Paralytic; Katavasias of Pascha, and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (Third Eothinon: Mark 16:9–20); then Ode 9 of Pascha with the Megalynaria and...
[^470]: Sunday of the Paralytic Concluded (§ 22) and The Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost Opening (§§ 23–24). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p821 (printed page 806) and facing English leaf p822 (printed page 807). Concluding rubrics of Sunday of the Paralytic Matins: At the Praises, four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 3 and the first Paschal Sticheron: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*); "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "O Lord, the Paralytic..." (*Κύριε τὸν Παράλυτον*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival, and let us embrace one another!..." (*Ἀναστάσεως ἡμέρα*); Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion: "Today is salvation come into the world: let us sing unto Him Who arose from the tomb, the Author of our life!..." (*Σήμερον σωτηρία*). Paragraph 22: Sunday of the Paralytic Divine Liturgy: Paschal Antiphons and the Entrance Hymn of Pascha. After the Entrance, the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 3: "Let the heavens rejoice, let earthly things be glad!..." (*Εὐφραινέσθω τὰ Οὐράνια*), that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One..." (*Εἰ καὶ ἐν τάφῳ*). The Epistle (Acts 9:32–42) and Gospel (John 5:1–15) of the Sunday. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace: O pure Virgin, rejoice, and again I say rejoice; for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day..." (*Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα*), with the Ninth Heirmos of the Canon: "With one voice, O Virgin, we bless thee: Hail, gate of the Lord, through which none may pass!..." (*Συμφώνως Παρθένε*) [Printed Note 12 of Violakis: Documents that it has become the customary practice on this Sunday and the following, perhaps for variety, to chant the Theotokia of the Canon instead of "Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem..."]. Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!" (*Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*), "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!" (*Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*), "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. The Service of the Paralytic is chanted until Tuesday, on which day its Apodosis is celebrated. Paragraph 23: The Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost Vespers: On Tuesday evening, after "Christ is risen from the dead...", we recite the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX). At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the three Prosomia: "Now has come the midpoint of the days..." (*Πάρεστιν ἡ μεσότης ἡμερῶν*) to six (twice each); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: "As the Feast comes to its midpoint, O Christ God..." (*Τῆς ἑορτῆς μεσούσης*); Entrance with censer, the Prokimenon of the day, and the Old Testament Readings (Micah 4:2–3, 5; 5:2–5, 8; Isaiah 55:1–3, 6–11; Proverbs 9:1–11 LXX). At the Aposticha: the three Idiomela: "The midpoint of the days of Pentecost has arrived..." (*Πεντηκοστῆς ἐφέστηκεν*), with the appointed Verses: "Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased from the beginning" (*Μνήσθητι τῆς συναγωγῆς σου ἧς ἐκτήσω ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς*, Ps. 73:2 LXX), and "God is our King before the ages: He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth" (*Ὁ δὲ Θεὸς Βασιλεὺς ἡμῶν πρὸ αἰώνων εἰργάσατο σωτηρίαν ἐν μέσῳ τῆς γῆς*, Ps. 73:12 LXX); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "At the midpoint of the Feast..." (*Μεσούσης τῆς ἑορτῆς*); Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8: "At the midpoint of the Feast, provide Thou my thirsty soul with the water of piety, as Thou didst cry unto all, O Savior: Whosoever thirsteth, let him come unto Me and drink! O Well-spring of our life, Christ God, glory to Thee!" (*Μεσούσης τῆς ἑορτῆς*) three times, and Dismissal. Paragraph 24: Wednesday Matins: After the Midnight Office, the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion, and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8 (three times); then the Psalter; then the two Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis of the Kathisma [Stichologia], twice each; then "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." (*Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι*) and Psalm 50 recited plainly. Both Canons without verses. After the 3rd Ode, the two Kathismata; after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 4: "As the Feast of the Law is at midpoint, O Creator and Master of all, Thou didst say unto those present, O Christ God: Come and draw forth the water of immortality!..." (*Τῆς ἑορτῆς τῆς νομικῆς μεσαζούσης*), the Ikos, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Feast. The Katavasias: "Thou didst make the sea a wall..." (*Θάλασσαν ἔπηξας*); then the 9th Ode of both Canons, and the Katavasia: "Virginity is alien to mothers, and childbearing is strange to virgins; yet in thee, O Theotokos, both were accomplished!..." (*Ἀλλότριον τῶν Μητέρων*); the Exaposteilarion: "Having arrived at the midpoint of the feasts..." (*Ὁ τὸν κρατῆρα ἔχων*) twice. At the Praises: three Prosomia to four (repeating the first); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 4: "Having been enlightened, brethren, by the Resurrection of Christ the Savior..." (*Φωτισθέντες ἀδελφοί*); Great Doxology and the Dismissal Troparion.
[^471]: Mid-Pentecost Concluded (§ 25) and The Fifth Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Samaritan Woman (§§ 26–28 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p823 (printed page 808) and facing English leaf p824 (printed page 809). Paragraph 25: Wednesday Divine Liturgy of Mid-Pentecost: Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn. After the Entrance, the Dismissal Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast; the Epistle (Acts 14:6–18) and Gospel (John 7:14–30) of the day. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Ninth Heirmos of Mid-Pentecost: "Virginity is alien to mothers, and childbearing is strange to virgins; yet in thee, O Theotokos, both were accomplished!..." (*Ἀλλότριον τῶν Μητέρων ἡ παρθενία*). Communion Hymn: "He who eats My flesh and drinks My blood abides in Me, and I in him. Alleluia!" (*Ὁ τρώγων μου τὴν σάρκα*, John 6:56), "Christ is risen from the dead...", "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 26: The Fifth Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Samaritan Woman Vespers: On Saturday evening, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Psalter (First Kathisma: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 4, three of Mid-Pentecost, and three of the Samaritan Woman; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "By Jacob's well Jesus found the Samaritan woman: He Who covers the earth with clouds asks water of her..." (*Παρὰ τὸ φρέαρ τοῦ Ἰακώβ*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 4: "The grandparent of God for Thy sake, David the Prophet, melodiously sang concerning thee unto Him Who wrought great things in thee..." (*Ὁ διὰ σὲ θεοπάτωρ*). At the Aposticha: the Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 4: "O Lord, when Thou didst ascend the Cross, Thou didst wipe away our ancestral curse..." (*Κύριε ἀνελθὼν ἐν τῷ Σταυρῷ*), then the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*) with their verses; "Glory...": of the Samaritan Woman, in Tone 8; "Both now and ever...": of the Feast, in Tone 8: "At the midpoint of the Feast..." (*Μεσούσης τῆς ἑορτῆς*); the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Having learned the joyful proclamation of the Resurrection from the angel..." (*Τὸ φαιδρὸν τῆς Ἀναστάσεως*), the Dismissal Troparion of the Feast in Tone 8: "At the midpoint of the Feast...", and Dismissal. Paragraph 27: Sunday of the Samaritan Woman Matins: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon in Tone 4, the "Glory... Both now and ever..." of the Liti: "Let heaven and earth exult today in joy, for Christ hath appeared in the flesh as man!..." (*Ἀγαλλιάσθω σήμερον φαιδρῶς*), and the Triadic hymn: "It is truly right..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*); then the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion: "At the midpoint of the Feast...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4 (twice) and that of the Feast (once); the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma). The Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis of the Kathisma [Stichologia], the Resurrectional Evlogitaria ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), the Ypakoe of Tone 4, the Anavathmoi of Tone 4, and the Prokimenon in Tone 4. The Canon of Pascha with the two Theotokia of Saint John of Damascus. After the 3rd Ode, the first Kathisma of the Feast, the second of the Samaritan Woman, and the third again of the Feast. After the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "Having come to the well in faith, the Samaritan woman beheld Thee, the Water of Wisdom; and having drunk abundantly, she inherited the heavenly kingdom, ever praised in songs!" (*Πίστει ἐλθοῦσα ἐν τῷ φρέατι*) and the Ikos of the Samaritan Woman, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman. The Katavasias of Pascha ("It is the day of Resurrection...") and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (Fourth Eothinon: Luke 24:1–12); then the Ninth Ode of Pascha with the Megalynaria together with the two Theotokia. The Exaposteilaria: of Pascha, of the Samaritan Woman, and of the Feast. At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 4, and the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..."; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "The well-spring of the beginning of life, Jesus our Savior, having come to the well of the Patriarch Jacob..." (*Ἡ πηγὴ τῆς ζωαρχίας*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival..."; Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion: "Today is salvation come into the world...". Paragraph 28 Opening: Divine Liturgy of the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman: Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn of Pascha. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4, that of the Feast (Mid-Pentecost), that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One..." (*Εἰ καὶ ἐν τάφῳ*). The Epistle (Acts 11:19–30) and Gospel (John 4:5–42) of the Sunday. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace: O pure Virgin, rejoice, and again I say rejoice; for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day...", with the Ninth Heirmos of the Canon: "Be glad, be joyful, O divine gate of the Light: for Jesus, having set into the grave, hath shone forth brighter than the sun..." (*Εὐφραίνου ἀγάλλου*) [Note 12]. Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!"...
[^472]: Sunday of the Samaritan Woman Concluded (§ 28), Weekdays of Mid-Pentecost Note, and The Sixth Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Blind Man (§§ 29–31 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p825 (printed page 810) and facing English leaf p826 (printed page 811). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 28 (Divine Liturgy of the Samaritan Woman): Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!" (*Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*), "Christ is risen from the dead...", "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. Rubrical Note on the Weekdays of Mid-Pentecost and Samaritan Woman: The Service of the Samaritan Woman is chanted only on Sunday, Thursday, Friday, and Saturday, on which day its Apodosis is celebrated. On Monday and Tuesday we chant the Afterfeast hymns of Mid-Pentecost. On Wednesday we celebrate the Apodosis of Mid-Pentecost, the Service being chanted as prescribed in Paragraphs 23–25, except for the Old Testament Readings. The Epistle and Gospel are of the day. Paragraph 29: The Sixth Sunday After Pascha: Sunday of the Blind Man Vespers: On Saturday evening, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Psalter (First Kathisma: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant seven Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 5, and three of the Blind Man; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "Lord, passing by on the way, Thou didst find a man blind from his birth..." (*Κύριε παράγων ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 5: "In the Red Sea there was once depicted an image of the unwedded bride..." (*Ἐν τῇ Ἐρυθρᾷ θαλάσσῃ*). At the Aposticha: the Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 5: "Thou Who didst take flesh, O Christ our Savior, and yet wast not parted from the heavens..." (*Σὲ τὸν σαρκωθέντα σωτῆρα Χριστόν*), then the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*) with their verses; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "O Sun of Righteousness, Christ God, Who by Thy pure touch didst grant light to both eyes of him who from his mother's womb was deprived of light..." (*Δικαιοσύνης Ἥλιε*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival..."; the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 5: "The Word co-eternal with the Father and the Spirit, Who for our salvation was born of the Virgin, let us faithful praise and worship!..." (*Τὸν συνάναρχον λόγον*), the Theotokion in Tone 5: "Hail, impassable Gate of the Lord!..." (*Χαῖρε Πύλη Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 30: Sunday of the Blind Man Matins: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon in Tone 5, the "Glory..." of the Liti, and "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "Incline to the entreaties of thy servants, O all-blameless one..." (*Νεῦσον παρακλήσεσι*), and the Triadic hymn: "It is truly right..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*); then the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion: "The Word co-eternal with the Father...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 5 (twice) and the Theotokion (once); the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma). The Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis of the Kathisma [Stichologia], the Resurrectional Evlogitaria ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), the Ypakoe of Tone 5, the Anavathmoi of Tone 5, and the Prokimenon in Tone 5. The Canon of Pascha with the two Theotokia of Saint John of Damascus. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma in Tone 8: "As the Master of all and the Fashioner, passing by on the way, Thou didst find a blind man seated and lamenting..." (*Ὁ τῶν ὅλων Δεσπότης*) (twice); after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 4: "My soul's eyes being blind, I come unto Thee, O Christ, as the man blind from birth, crying unto Thee in repentance: Thou art the most radiant Light of those in darkness!" (*Τῆς ψυχῆς τὰ ὄμματα*) and the Ikos of the Blind Man, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Sunday of the Blind Man. The Katavasias: "Let us sing unto the Savior God, Who led the people through the sea with dry feet..." (*Τῷ Σωτῆρι Θεῷ*) and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (Fifth Eothinon: Luke 24:12–35); then the Ninth Ode of Pascha with the Megalynaria together with the two Theotokia, and the Katavasia: "Thee who art beyond human mind and word, who hast given birth in time unto the Timeless One, the faithful with one accord do magnify!" (*Σὲ τὴν ὑπὲρ νοῦν*). The Exaposteilarion of Pascha: "Having slept in the flesh as a mortal..." (*Σαρκὶ ὑπνώσας ὡς θνητός*) and the two Exaposteilaria of the Blind Man: "Passing by, our Savior found the blind man..." (*Παράγων ὁ Σωτὴρ ἡμῶν*). At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 5, and the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*); "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "Who can tell of Thy mighty acts, O Christ, or who can recount the multitudes of Thy wonders?..." (*Τίς λαλήσει τὰς δυναστείας σου Χριστέ*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival..."; Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion: "Today is salvation come into the world..." (*Σήμερον σωτηρία*). Paragraph 31 Opening: Divine Liturgy of the Sunday of the Blind Man: Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn of Pascha. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 5, that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One..." (*Εἰ καὶ ἐν τάφῳ*). The Epistle (Acts 16:16–34) and Gospel (John 9:1–38) of the Sunday...
[^473]: Sunday of the Blind Man Concluded (§ 31) and The Apodosis of the Blind Man (§§ 32–34). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p827 (printed page 812) and facing English leaf p828 (printed page 813). Concluding rubrics of Paragraph 31 (Divine Liturgy of the Blind Man): At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace: O pure Virgin, rejoice, and again I say rejoice; for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day. Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem, for the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee!..." (*Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα . . . Φωτίζου, φωτίζου*). Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!" (*Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*), the Dismissal Troparion: "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!" (*Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*), "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 32: Monday evening: The Apodosis of the Service of the Blind Man Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the six Idiomela from the Pentekostarion which are indicated in the Vespers of Tuesday evening, in order; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "Who can tell of Thy mighty acts, O Christ?..." (*Τίς λαλήσει τὰς δυναστείας σου Χριστέ*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 5: "In the Red Sea there was once depicted an image of the unwedded bride..." (*Ἐν τῇ Ἐρυθρᾷ θαλάσσῃ*). At the Aposticha: the four Saturday evening Stichera of the Octoechos in Tone 5: "By Thy precious Cross hast Thou put the devil to shame, O Christ..." (*Διὰ τοῦ Τιμίου σου Σταυροῦ*), "He Who has granted resurrection unto the human race..." (*Ὁ τὴν Ἀνάστασιν διδούς*), "O great wonder! The Creator of invisible things..." (*Μέγα θαῦμα*), and the Apostichon: "Thou Who didst take flesh, O Christ our Savior..." (*Σὲ τὸν σαρκωθέντα σωτῆρα Χριστόν*), with the verses: "The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty!" (*Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*, Ps. 92:1 LXX) etc.; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "O Sun of Righteousness, Christ God..." (*Δικαιοσύνης Ἥλιε*). The Dismissal Troparion in Tone 5: "The Word co-eternal with the Father and the Spirit..." (*Τὸν συνάναρχον λόγον*), the Theotokion in Tone 5: "Hail, impassable Gate of the Lord!..." (*Χαῖρε Πύλη Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. Paragraph 33: Tuesday Matins of the Apodosis of the Blind Man: After the Midnight Office, the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion, and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 5 (twice) and the Theotokion (once), then the Psalter. The Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis of the Kathisma [Stichologia], which are indicated on Wednesday morning; then we recite "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." (*Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι*), Psalm 50, and the Sunday Canon: "Having accepted a voluntary crucifixion, O Master..." (*Σταύρωσιν ἑκούσιον*), with the Verse: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!" (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι*), and the Forefeast Canon: "Up unto the Father hast Thou ascended, O Christ God..." (*Ἄνω πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα*) with the same Verse. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma in Tone 4: "Thou didst give eyes to him who was blind from birth, O Christ..." (*Ὠμμάτωσας Χριστέ*) and the other one: "Thou wast born as such, O blind man..." (*Ἐτέχθης ὡς αὐτός*). After the 6th Ode, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Sunday of the Blind Man, and the Menologion of the Saints of the day. The Katavasias: "Let us sing unto the Savior God..." (*Τῷ Σωτῆρι Θεῷ*); and we chant "More honorable than the Cherubim..." (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν τῶν Χερουβίμ*) with the Katavasia: "Thee who art beyond human mind and word..." (*Σὲ τὴν ὑπὲρ νοῦν*). The two Exaposteilaria of the Blind Man. At the Praises: three Resurrectional Stichera of the Sunday in Tone 5, and the first three Vespers hymns of the Blind Man; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "Who can tell of Thy mighty acts, O Christ?..." (*Τίς λαλήσει τὰς δυναστείας σου Χριστέ*). Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion: "The Word co-eternal with the Father..." (*Τὸν συνάναρχον λόγον*). Paragraph 34: Divine Liturgy of the Apodosis: The Paschal Antiphons and Entrance Hymn of Pascha. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 5, that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion in Tone 4: "My soul's eyes being blind, I come unto Thee, O Christ..." (*Τῆς ψυχῆς τὰ ὄμματα*). The Epistle (Acts 16:20–34) and Gospel (John 9:39–10:9) of the day. At "Especially", "It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos, ever-blessed and most pure, and the Mother of our God!..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*), the Communion Hymn of the day: "Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest! Alleluia!" (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*, Ps. 148:1 LXX), the Dismissal Troparion: "Christ is risen from the dead..." (*Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*), "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal.
[^474]: Wednesday of the 6th Week: The Apodosis of the Feast of Pascha (§§ 35–36). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p829 (printed page 814) and facing English leaf p830 (printed page 815). Paragraph 35: Tuesday evening: The Ninth Hour is read as tri-psalmic (involving Psalms 83, 84, 85 LXX), at which we say the Dismissal Troparion: "The Word co-eternal with the Father and the Spirit..." (*Τὸν συνάναρχον λόγον*), and the Kontakion: "My soul's eyes being blind, I come unto Thee, O Christ..." (*Τῆς ψυχῆς τὰ ὄμματα*); then, instead of the usual "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", the Priest exclaims (as is the custom now): "Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and indivisible Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (*Δόξα τῇ ἁγίᾳ καὶ ὁμοουσίῳ*). Then the Priest inside the Sanctuary chants "Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and upon those in the tombs bestowing life!" (*Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*) three times; this hymn is chanted also three times by each of the two Choirs, and again immediately once by the Priest from the Sanctuary [Note: The rubrics of the Pentekostarion state simply: "And we chant Christ is risen with its verses, exactly as was done on the day of Pascha." Apparently, this exact order is what is actually being called for here: if each of the two Choirs chants "Christ is risen" three times, we have the sum total of 10 times, as on the day of Pascha]. Then the Litany follows. At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the six Stichera of the Octoechos in Tone 5: "Accept our evening prayers, O holy Lord, and grant unto us remission of sins; for Thou alone art He Who didst manifest the Resurrection unto the world!" (*Τὰς ἑσπερινὰς ἡμῶν εὐχὰς*) in order; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 5: "Him Who is co-eternal with the Father and the Spirit, let us praise..." (*Τὸν τῷ Πατρὶ συνάναρχον*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 1: "The Joy of all the world, who sprang forth from mankind and gave birth unto the Master..." (*Τὴν παγκόσμιον δόξαν*). Entrance with censer, and the Prokimenon of the day: "Thy mercy, O Lord, shall pursue me all the days of my life!" (*Τὸ ἔλεός σου, Κύριε*, Ps. 22:6 LXX). At the Aposticha: the Resurrectional Apostichon Sticheron in Tone 5: "By Thy Passion, O Christ, hast Thou darkened the sun, and by the light of Thy Resurrection hast Thou enlightened all things..." (*Τῷ πάθει σου Χριστέ*), then the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*) with their verses; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival, and let us embrace one another!..." (*Ἀναστάσεως ἡμέρα*), then "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times), and Dismissal. Paragraph 36: Wednesday morning Matins of the Apodosis of Pascha: After the Midnight Office of Pascha (see Paragraph 2, Note 8), the Priest censing in front of the Holy Table exclaims: "Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and indivisible Trinity, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", and chants "Christ is risen from the dead..." (*Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*) as yesterday; then the Litany, and the Paschal Canon is chanted with four Troparia for each ode including the Katavasia. Then we chant "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times), the hymn: "Jesus, having arisen from the tomb, as He foretold, hath given unto us life eternal and great mercy!" (*Ἀναστὰς ὁ Ἰησοῦς ἀπὸ τοῦ τάφου*), the Litany, and the Exclamation at the end of each Ode. After the 3rd Ode, the Ypakoe: "Coming before the dawn, Mary and the women found the stone rolled away from the tomb..." (*Προλαβοῦσαι τὸν Ὄρθρον*) (recited plainly); after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One..." and the Ikos of Pascha, then "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship the holy Lord Jesus..." (*Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι*) (three times), and "Jesus, having arisen from the tomb..." (*Ἀναστὰς ὁ Ἰησοῦς*); then the 7th and 8th Odes of the Canon are chanted. Then the deacon or priest proclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light, honoring her in hymns, let us magnify!" (*Τὴν Θεοτόκον καὶ Μητέρα τοῦ Φωτός*), and the 9th Ode is chanted with the Megalynaria of Pascha. The Exaposteilarion: "Having slept in the flesh as a mortal, O King and Lord, on the third day Thou didst arise..." (*Σαρκὶ ὑπνώσας ὡς θνητός*) (three times). At the Praises: the four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 1: "We praise Thy saving Passion, O Christ, and glorify Thy Resurrection!" (*Ὑμνοῦμέν σου Χριστέ*) and the Stichera of Pascha: "A sacred Pascha is shown forth unto us today..." (*Πάσχα ἱερόν*) with their verses; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 5: "It is the day of Resurrection: let us be radiant for the festival..." (*Ἀναστάσεως ἡμέρα*), then "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times), and the Divine Liturgy begins.
[^475]: Apodosis of Pascha Liturgy (§ 37) and Thursday of the Ascension: Vespers (§ 38) and Matins (§ 39 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p831 (printed page 816) and facing English leaf p832 (printed page 817). Concluding rubrics of the Apodosis of Pascha: Divine Liturgy of Pascha with Paschal Antiphons, Entrance Hymn, Dismissal Troparion: "Christ is risen from the dead..." (*Χριστὸς ἀνέστη*), the Ypakoe, and Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave, O Immortal One..." (*Εἰ καὶ ἐν τάφῳ*). In place of the Trisagion Hymn: "As many as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia!" (*Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε*). Epistle (Acts 18:22–28) and Gospel (John 12:36–47) of the day. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: "The Angel cried unto her who is full of grace: O pure Virgin, rejoice, and again I say rejoice; for thy Son has arisen from the grave on the third day. Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem, for the glory of the Lord is risen upon thee!..." (*Ὁ Ἄγγελος ἐβόα . . . Φωτίζου, φωτίζου*). Communion Hymn of Pascha: "Receive the Body of Christ; taste of the immortal Fountain. Alleluia!" (*Σῶμα Χριστοῦ*), "Christ is risen from the dead...". After the Prayer behind the Amvon, "Christ is risen from the dead..." (three times) recited plainly, Dismissal, and Apodosis. Paragraph 38: The Thursday of the Ascension: Great Vespers: On Wednesday evening, following the Ninth Hour of Pascha, the Priest intones "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." and the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) begins with "Come, let us worship and fall down before Christ, our King and our God" (three times). At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the five Idiomela: "The Lord ascended into the heavens, that He might send the Paraclete unto the world..." (*Ὁ Κύριος ἀνελήφθη*) to six, repeating the first hymn; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: "Without parting from the Fatherly bosom, O sweetest Jesus..." (*Τῶν κόλπων τῶν Πατρικῶν*). Entrance with censer, the Prokimenon of the day: "Save me, O God, by Thy name, and judge me by Thy power!" (*Ὁ Θεὸς ἐν τῷ ὀνόματί σου σῶσόν με*, Ps. 53:1 LXX), and the Old Testament Readings (Isaiah 2:2–3; 62:10–63:3, 7–9; Zechariah 14:1, 4, 8–11 LXX). At the Aposticha: the three Idiomela: "Thou wast born as Thou Thyself didst will..." (*Ἐτέχθης ὡς αὐτὸς ἠθέλησας*) with their verses; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: "God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet..." (*Ἀνέβη ὁ Θεὸς ἐν ἀλαλαγμῷ*). The Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God, having gladdened Thy disciples with the promise of the Holy Spirit, they having been assured by the blessing that Thou art the Son of God, the Redeemer of the world!" (*Ἀνελήφθης ἐν δόξῃ*) three times, and the Dismissal with the appointed prologue: "He Who ascended in glory from us into the heavens and sat at the right hand of God the Father, Christ our true God...". Paragraph 39: Thursday Matins of the Ascension: After Psalm 50, the Liti, then the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Thou hast ascended in glory...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparion (three times); then the Psalter and the Polyeleos. The Kathismata in order, twice each; the Anavathmoi: the first Antiphon of Tone 4 ("From my youth many passions war against me..."), and the Prokimenon: "God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet!" (*Ἀνέβη ὁ Θεὸς ἐν ἀλαλαγμῷ*, Ps. 46:5 LXX), "Let everything that hath breath praise the Lord!" (*Πᾶσα πνοὴ*), and the Third Matins Gospel (Mark 16:9–20), "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." (*Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι*), and Psalm 50 recited plainly. "Glory...": "Through the intercessions of the Apostles..."; "Both now and ever...": "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos..."; the Verse: "Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy...", and the Idiomelon: "Today the heavenly powers in the heavens marvel seeing our nature ascend..." (*Σήμερον ἐν Οὐρανοῖς αἱ ἄνω δυνάμεις*). Both Canons, the first to six and the second to four, without verses. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma (once); after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 6: "When Thou hadst fulfilled the dispensation for our sakes, and united things on earth with the heavenly, Thou didst ascend in glory, O Christ our God..." (*Τὴν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν πληρώσας*), the Ikos, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Feast. The Katavasias: "Covered by a divine cloud, he that was slow of tongue proclaimed the Law written of God..." (*Θείῳ καλυφθείς*); then the 9th Ode is chanted: the first Canon without verses, and the second Canon with its Megalynarion: "Ye Angels, marveling at the ascension of the Master, how with glory He was taken up from earth unto the heights..." (*Ἄγγελοι τὴν ἄνοδον τοῦ Δεσπότου . . . πῶς μετὰ δόξης ἐπήρθη ἀπὸ τῆς γῆς εἰς τὰ ἄνω*); the Katavasia: "Rejoice, O Queen, boasting of virgins and mothers!..." (*Χαίροις Ἄνασσα*), then the Exaposteilarion: "While the disciples looked on, Thou wast taken up, O Christ, unto the Father, to sit beside Him..." (*Τῶν Μαθητῶν ὁρώντων σε*) (three times). At the Praises: the three Prosomia to four (repeating the first); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 2: "Thou wast born as Thou Thyself didst will; Thou didst appear as Thou Thyself didst wish; Thou didst suffer in the flesh, O our God..." (*Ἐτέχθης ὡς αὐτὸς ἠθέλησας*)...

Concluding rubrics of Ascension Matins: Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God..." (*Ἀνελήφθης ἐν δόξῃ*). Paragraph 40: Divine Liturgy of the Ascension of the Lord: The Appointed Antiphons: First Antiphon (Psalm 46 LXX: "Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing...", Refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!"); Second Antiphon (Psalm 47 LXX: "Great is the Lord, and greatly to be praised, in the city of our God, in His holy mountain...", Refrain: "Save us, O Son of God, Who didst ascend in glory from us into the heavens, save us who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!", and "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Only-begotten Son and Word of God, Who art immortal..."); Third Antiphon (Psalm 48 LXX: "Hear this, all ye nations; give ear, all ye inhabitants of the world!...", Refrain: Dismissal Troparion: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God..."). The Entrance Hymn: "God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet! Save us, O Son of God, Who didst ascend in glory from us into the heavens, save us who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!" (*Ἀνέβη ὁ Θεὸς ἐν ἀλαλαγμῷ, Κύριος ἐν φωνῇ σάλπιγγος· Σῶσον ἡμᾶς Υἱὲ Θεοῦ, ὁ ἐν δόξῃ ἀναληφθείς...*). The Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God...", the Kontakion in Tone 6: "When Thou hadst fulfilled the dispensation for our sakes, and united things on earth with the heavenly, Thou didst ascend in glory, O Christ our God, not being parted from us, but remaining unseparated, and crying unto them that love Thee: I am with you, and no one can be against you!" (*Τὴν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν πληρώσας*).
[^477]: Ascension Liturgy Concluded (§ 40) and The Seventh Sunday: Holy Fathers of the First Synod at Nicea (§§ 41–42). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p835 (printed page 820) and facing English leaf p836 (printed page 821). Concluding rubrics of Ascension Liturgy: The Epistle (Acts 1:1–12) and Gospel (Luke 24:36–53). At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Ninth Heirmos of the Feast: "Thee who art beyond human mind and word, who hast given birth in time unto the Timeless One, the faithful with one accord do magnify!" (*Σὲ τὴν ὑπὲρ νοῦν*). Communion Hymn: "God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet. Alleluia!" (*Ἀνέβη ὁ Θεὸς ἐν ἀλαλαγμῷ*, Ps. 46:5 LXX). After the Communion Hymn, the Dismissal Troparion: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God..."; "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and the Dismissal as in Vespers [Printed Note 13 of Violakis: Documents that the exact order of the Dismissal is given as stated]. Paragraph 41: The Seventh Sunday: The Holy Fathers of the First Ecumenical Council at Nicea: Great Vespers: On Saturday evening, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Psalter (First Kathisma: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant three Resurrectional Stichera of Tone 6, three of the Ascension, and four of the Fathers; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "Let us extol today the mystical trumpets of the Spirit, the God-bearing Fathers..." (*Τὰς μυστικὰς σήμερον τοῦ Πνεύματος σάλπιγγας*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 6: "Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?..." (*Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε*). Entrance with censer, "O Joyful Light" (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), the Prokimenon of the day: "The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty!" (*Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*, Ps. 92:1 LXX), and the Old Testament Readings (Genesis 14:14–20; Deuteronomy 1:8–11, 15–17; 10:14–21 LXX). At the Aposticha: the Resurrectional Stichera; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 4: "Let us faithfully celebrate today the yearly commemoration of the God-bearing Fathers..." (*Τὴν ἐτήσιον μνήμην σήμερον*); "Both now and ever...": of the Feast, in Tone 4: "O Lord, having fulfilled the mystery hidden from the ages..." (*Κύριε τὸ μυστήριον*). The Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion of Tone 6, "Glory...": that of the Fathers in Tone 8: "Most glorified art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast established our Fathers as luminous stars upon the earth..." (*Ὑπερδεδοξασμένος εἶ, Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*); "Both now and ever...": that of the Ascension in Tone 4: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God...", and Dismissal. Paragraph 42: Sunday Matins of the Holy Fathers: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon in Tone 6, the "Glory..." of the Liti: "Keeping the Apostolic traditions..." (*Ἀποστολικῶν παραδόσεων*); "Both now and ever...": of the Feast: "O Lord, having fulfilled the mystery of the dispensation..." (*Κύριε τῆς οἰκονομίας πληρώσας τὸ μυστήριον*); then the Triadic hymns: "It is truly right..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*), the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion of the Fathers: "Most glorified art Thou, O Christ our God...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparia as in Vespers; then the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma). The Resurrectional Kathismata in order, the Resurrectional Evlogitaria ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), the Ypakoe of Tone 6, the Anavathmoi of Tone 6, and the Prokimenon of Tone 6. The Canons: the Resurrectional Canon, that of the Feast, and that of the Fathers. After the 3rd Ode [Translator's Note: We recite the Kontakion of the Ascension: "When Thou hadst fulfilled the dispensation for our sakes...". Note that this is indicated both in the Pentekostarion and in the Imerologia [IEP] 1998 and 1999; though the Typikon itself is not clear on this issue, it appears that a look at the Protheoria (Violakis, p. 22, § 25) supports this practice], then the Kathisma of the Fathers and that of the Feast. After the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "The preaching of the Apostles and the dogmas of the Fathers confirmed the one Faith for the Church..." (*Τῶν Ἀποστόλων τὸ κήρυγμα*) and the Ikos of the Fathers, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Sunday of the Fathers. The Katavasias: "Covered by a divine cloud..." (*Θείῳ καλυφθείς*), and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (Tenth Eothinon: John 21:1–14); then we chant with verses: "More honorable than the Cherubim..." (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν τῶν Χερουβίμ*). Exaposteilaria: the Resurrectional Exaposteilarion, that of the Fathers, and that of the Feast. At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 6 and four of the Fathers; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "The choir of the Holy Fathers, coming together from the ends of the earth..." (*Τῶν Ἁγίων Πατέρων ὁ χορός*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 2: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..." (*Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*). Great Doxology and Dismissal Troparion: "Today is salvation come into the world..." (*Σήμερον σωτηρία*).
[^478]: Seventh Sunday Liturgy (§ 43), Apodosis of Ascension Note, and Saturday of the Seventh Week: Memorial Saturday (§ 44 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p837 (printed page 822) and facing English leaf p838 (printed page 823). Paragraph 43: Sunday Divine Liturgy of the Holy Fathers: Typika and Beatitudes of Tone 6 to four, and four hymns from the 6th Ode of the Canon of the Fathers. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion of Tone 6, that of the Feast (Ascension), that of the Fathers, and that of the Patron Saint of the Church; the Kontakion: "When Thou hadst fulfilled the dispensation for our sakes..." (*Τὴν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν πληρώσας*). The Epistle (Acts 20:16–18, 28–36) and Gospel (John 17:1–13) of the Sunday. At "Especially", "It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*). Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest! Alleluia!" (*Αἰνεῖτε τὸν Κύριον*, Ps. 148:1 LXX). At "With the fear of God...", we chant the Dismissal Troparion of the Ascension: "Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God...". Then "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. Rubrical Note on the Apodosis of the Ascension: On Friday of the seventh week the Apodosis of the Feast of the Ascension takes place; the Service is sung exactly as prescribed in Paragraphs 38–40: in Vespers, with the exception of the Old Testament Readings; and in Matins, with the exception of the Liti, the Polyeleos with its Kathisma of the Third Stasis, and also of the Matins Gospel. In the Divine Liturgy, we read the Epistle and Gospel of the day. Paragraph 44: The Saturday Before Pentecost: Memorial Saturday (Soul Saturday): Commemoration of all pious Christians reposed from the ages: On Friday evening, at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant six Stichera: four Martyrika from the Octoechos: "Thy Martyrs, O Lord..." (*Οἱ Μάρτυρές σου Κύριε*), and three for the Reposed: "Today, those who have died from time immemorial..." (*Τῶν ἀπ᾿ αἰῶνος σήμερον νεκρῶν*); "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "I weep and I lament when I contemplate death..." (*Θρηνῶ καὶ ὀδύρομαι*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 8: "Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?..." (*Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε*). In place of the Prokimenon, we chant "Alleluia" three times, with the appointed verses chanted for the last two times: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord" (*Μακάριοι οὓς ἐξελέξω καὶ προσελάβου, Κύριε*, Ps. 64:4 LXX) and "Their memorial is unto generation and generation" (*Καὶ τὸ μνημόσυνον αὐτῶν εἰς γενεὰν καὶ γενεάν*, Ps. 101:13 LXX). At the Aposticha: the Martyrikon: "Thy Cross, O Lord..." (*Ὁ Σταυρός σου Κύριε*), and the two Stichera of the Reposed with their verses: "Their souls shall dwell with good things" (*Αἱ ψυχαὶ αὐτῶν ἐν ἀγαθοῖς αὐλισθήσονται*, Ps. 24:13 LXX). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "My beginning and foundation was Thy creative command..." (*Ἀρχή μοι καὶ ὑπόστασις*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: "By the intercessions of her who gave birth unto Thee, O Christ..." (*Πρεσβείαις τῆς τεκούσης σε*). The prayer "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace..." (*Νῦν ἀπολύεις*), and immediately we chant the Canon: "In the heavenly bridal chambers..." (*Ἐν οὐρανίοις θαλάμοις*) with the Verses: "Through the intercessions of Thy Martyrs, O Christ God, give rest to the souls of Thy servants!" (*Πρεσβείαις τῶν Μαρτύρων σου, Χριστὲ ὁ Θεός, ἀνάπαυσον τὰς ψυχὰς τῶν δούλων σου*) and "Their souls shall dwell with good things", chanting "Glory...", "Both now and ever..." in each Ode. After the 9th Ode, the Heirmos, the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8: "O Thou Who with the depth of wisdom..." (*Ὁ βάθει σοφίας*); "Glory...": the ending: "For in Thee have they placed their hope, our Maker and Creator and God" (*Ἐν σοὶ γὰρ τὴν ἐλπίδα*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "Thee do we have as a rampart and haven..." (*Σὲ καὶ τεῖχος καὶ λιμένα ἔχομεν*); then the Troparia: "With the souls of the righteous departed, give rest to the soul of Thy servant, O Savior..." (*Μετὰ πνευμάτων δικαίων*), and the names of the Reposed are commemorated over the Kolyvo [boiled wheat], as noted in the Triodion...

Paragraph 45: Saturday Matins of the Reposed: After the Midnight Office, the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion: "O Thou Who with the depth of wisdom..." (*Ὁ βάθει σοφίας*), and the Six Psalms; then "Alleluia" with its verses and the Dismissal Troparia, as prescribed on the Saturday before Meatfare Sunday; the Psalter, the Kathismata, and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma), and the names of the Reposed are commemorated as yesterday at Vespers; the Evlogitaria of the Reposed ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), and after the Exclamation, the Kathisma: "Give rest with the righteous, O our Savior, unto Thy servants..." (*Ἀνάπαυσον Σωτὴρ ἡμῶν*); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "O Thou Who didst dawn from the Virgin..." (*Ὁ ἐκ Παρθένου ἀνατείλας*). Then Psalm 50, and the Canon: "O Master of all souls..." (*Ψυχῶν πασῶν ὁ Δεσπότης*) with the prescribed verses. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma and the Theotokion. After the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "With the Saints grant rest, O Christ, unto the souls of Thy servants, where there is neither pain, nor grief, nor sighing, but life everlasting!" (*Μετὰ τῶν Ἁγίων ἀνάπαυσον*), the Ikos, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Triodion; then we chant the Heirmos of the 8th Ode: "Be astonished and tremble, O heaven..." (*Ἔκστηθι φρίττων Οὐρανέ*), we chant with verses: "More honorable than the Cherubim..." (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν τῶν Χερουβίμ*), the Heirmos: "Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee as is meet..." (*Ἀπορεῖ πᾶσα γλῶσσα*), and the two Exaposteilaria. At the Praises: the four Stichera: "Dreaded is the end, the coming of death..." (*Φοβερὸν τὸ τέλος τὸ τοῦ θανάτου*); "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "As a flower fadeth, and as a shadow passeth away, so is every man dissolved..." (*Ὡς ἄνθος μαραίνεται*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 8: "Thou art our God, Who in wisdom hast fashioned all things..." (*Σὺ εἶ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*); the prayer "To Thee belongeth glory..." (*Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει*) recited plainly, the Litany, and the four Aposticha: "Having an inconceivable depth of compassions, O Christ..." (*Ἔχων ἀκατάληπτον*); "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "A pain unto Adam was the eating of the tree of old in Eden..." (*Ἄλγος τῷ Ἀδάμ*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: "Thou hast been seen as a dwelling-place, O unwedded Virgin..." (*Ὤφθης ἐνδιαίτημα*); then "It is good to give praise unto the Lord, and to chant unto Thy name, O Most High" (*Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*, Ps. 91:1 LXX); the Trisagion, the two Dismissal Troparia: "O Thou Who with the depth of wisdom..." (*Ὁ βάθει σοφίας*) and "Thee do we have as a rampart and haven..." (*Σὲ καὶ τεῖχος καὶ λιμένα ἔχομεν*). Paragraph 46: Memorial Saturday Divine Liturgy: The Antiphons: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord..." (*Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*). After the Entrance, the Dismissal Troparion: "O Thou Who with the depth of wisdom..." (*Ὁ βάθει σοφίας*), that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion: "With the Saints grant rest, O Christ..." (*Μετὰ τῶν Ἁγίων ἀνάπαυσον*) (see the 6th Ode of the Vespers Canon). The Epistle of the Reposed (1 Thessalonians 4:13–17) and Gospel of the day (John 21:15–25). At "Especially", "It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*). Communion Hymn: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord; and their memorial is unto generation and generation. Alleluia!" (*Μακάριοι οὓς ἐξελέξω*, Ps. 64:4 LXX). In place of "We have seen the true Light..." (*Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*), we chant the Dismissal Troparion: "O Thou Who with the depth of wisdom providing for all with love of mankind..." (*Ὁ βάθει σοφίας φιλανθρώπως πάντα οἰκονομῶν*) [Printed Note 14 of Violakis: In the printed edition of 1888, the Dismissal Troparion is sung here in place of "We have seen the true Light..."]; then we chant "With the spirits of the righteous departed..." (*Μετὰ πνευμάτων δικαίων*), and the names of all the deceased pious and Orthodox Christians from time immemorial are commemorated, etc., and Dismissal. Paragraph 47: The Sunday of Pentecost: Great Vespers: On Saturday evening, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX), we do not chant the Psalter [Translator's Note: The Pentekostarion prescribes the First Kathisma of the Psalter: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX]. At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we set ten verses and chant the three Idiomela of Tone 1: "We celebrate Pentecost and the coming of the Spirit..." (*Πεντηκοστὴν ἑορτάζομεν*) to five...
[^480]: Pentecost Vespers Concluded (§ 47), Pentecost Sunday Matins (§ 48), and Opening of Divine Liturgy Antiphon 1 (§ 49 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p841 (printed page 826) and facing English leaf p842 (printed page 827). Paragraph 47: Concluding rubrics of Great Vespers of Pentecost: At "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the three Idiomela of Tone 1 to five (repeating the first and second hymns), and the five of Tone 2: "We have seen the true Light..." (*Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*) to six (repeating the first hymn); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "Come, all ye peoples, let us worship the Godhead in three Persons..." (*Δεῦτε λαοί*). Entrance with censer, "O Joyful Light" (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), the Prokimenon of the day: "The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty!" (*Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*, Ps. 92:1 LXX), and the Old Testament Readings (Numbers 11:16–17, 24–29; Joel 2:23–32; Ezekiel 36:24–28 LXX). At the Aposticha: the three Idiomela: "The nations, being ignorant of the power of the All-holy Spirit..." (*Ἀγνοοῦντα τὰ ἔθνη, Κύριε*); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "Once the tongues were confused on account of the audacity of the tower-building..." (*Γλῶσσαί ποτε*). The Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen to be most wise, having sent down upon them the Holy Spirit, and through them caught the universe in a net; O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee!" (*Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*) three times, and the Dismissal with the appointed festal prologue: "May He Who sent down the All-holy Spirit from heaven in the form of fiery tongues upon His holy Disciples and Apostles, Christ our true God..." (*Ὁ ἐν εἴδει πυρίνων γλωσσῶν οὐρανόθεν καταπέμψας τὸ πανάγιον Πνεῦμα ἐπὶ τοὺς ἁγίους αὐτοῦ Μαθητὰς καὶ Ἀποστόλους, Χριστὸς ὁ ἀληθινὸς Θεὸς ἡμῶν...*). Paragraph 48: Sunday Matins of Pentecost: After Psalm 50, the Liti, then the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparion (three times); then the Psalter and the Polyeleos; the three Kathismata twice each; the Anavathmoi: the first Antiphon of Tone 4 ("From my youth many passions war against me..."), and the Prokimenon: "Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the land of uprightness!" (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθὸν ὁδηγήσει με ἐν γῇ εὐθείᾳ*, Ps. 142:10 LXX), "Let everything that hath breath praise the Lord!" (*Πᾶσα πνοὴ*), and the Matins Gospel: "When it was evening on that day..." (*Οὔσης ὀψίας*) until "whose soever sins ye retain, they are retained" (John 20:19–23). We do not recite "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (*Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ θεασάμενοι*), but read immediately Psalm 50 recited plainly. "Glory...": "Through the intercessions of the Apostles..." (*Ταῖς τῶν Ἀποστόλων*); "Both now and ever...": "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos..." (*Ταῖς τῆς Θεοτόκου*); the Verse: "Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy..." (*Ἐλέησόν με ὁ Θεός*), and the Idiomelon in Tone 6: "O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of good things and Giver of life: come and dwell in us, and cleanse us from every stain, and save our souls, O Good One!" (*Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*). Both Canons without verses. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma (once); after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "When the Most High came down and confused the tongues, He divided the nations; but when He distributed the tongues of fire, He called all to unity; and with one voice we glorify the All-holy Spirit!" (*Ὅτε καταβὰς τὰς γλώσσας συνέχεε*), the Ikos, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of Pentecost. The Katavasias of both Canons [double] (both the Iambic and Heirmologic Katavasias); then the 9th Ode is chanted. The first Exaposteilarion: "O All-holy Spirit, Light proceeding from the Father, through the Son Thou didst come unto the unlettered Disciples..." (*Φῶς ὁ Πατήρ, φῶς ὁ Λόγος, φῶς καὶ τὸ ἅγιον Πνεῦμα*) (twice), and the second: "Our Light and Life is the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit..." (*Τριαδικὸν*) (once). At the Praises: the three Idiomela: "Marvelous things today have all the nations seen..." (*Παράδοξα σήμερον πάντα τὰ ἔθνη*) to four, repeating the first hymn [Note: The Pentekostarion indicates that each Idiomelon is repeated]; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: "O Heavenly King, Comforter..." (*Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*). The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal Troparion: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...". Paragraph 49: Divine Liturgy of Pentecost: The Appointed Antiphons: First Antiphon (Psalm 18 LXX: "The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaimeth the work of His hands...", Refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us!").
[^481]: Pentecost Divine Liturgy Continued (§ 49 Antiphons 2–3, Entrance, Readings, and Zadostoinyk). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p843 (printed page 828) and facing English leaf p844 (printed page 829). Continuation of Paragraph 49: Second Antiphon (Psalm 19 LXX: "The Lord hear thee in the day of affliction; the name of the God of Jacob defend thee...", Refrain: "Save us, O Good Paraclete, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!", and "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Only-begotten Son and Word of God, Who art immortal..."); Third Antiphon (Psalm 20 LXX: "In Thy strength the king shall be glad, O Lord, and in Thy salvation how greatly shall he exult!...", Refrain: Dismissal Troparion: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...") [Printed Note of Violakis: While this last verse ("He asked life of Thee, and Thou gavest him length of days unto the ages of ages") is not indicated in the Typikon, it is found in the Pentekostarion, the Horologion, and the Imerologia of the Patriarchate]. The Entrance Hymn: "Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will sing and praise Thy mighty acts! Save us, O Good Paraclete, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!" (*Ὑψώθητι Κύριε ἐν τῇ δυνάμει σου, ᾄσομεν καὶ ψαλοῦμεν τὰς δυναστείας σου· Σῶσον ἡμᾶς, Παράκλητε ἀγαθέ, τοὺς ψάλλοντάς σοι, Ἀλληλούϊα*, Ps. 20:13 LXX). After the Entrance, the Dismissal Troparion: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...", and the Kontakion: "When the Most High came down and confused the tongues...". Instead of the Trisagion Hymn, we chant: "As many as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia!" (*Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε*). The Epistle (Acts 2:1–11) and the Gospel (John 7:37–52; 8:12). At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Ninth Heirmos of the Iambic Canon in Tone 4: "Without experiencing corruption didst thou conceive, and didst lend flesh unto the Word, the Artificer of all, O Mother unwedded, Virgin Theotokos, vessel of the Uncontainable, dwelling-place of thine Infinite Creator: thee do we magnify!" (*Μὴ τῆς φθορᾶς διαπείρᾳ κυοφορήσασα*).
[^482]: Pentecost Liturgy Concluded (§ 49) and Great Vespers of Pentecost with the Kneeling Prayers (§ 50 Opening and Kneeling Prayers). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p845 (printed page 830) and facing English leaf p846 (printed page 831). Concluding rubrics of Pentecost Divine Liturgy: Communion Hymn: "Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the land of uprightness. Alleluia!" (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθὸν ὁδηγήσει με ἐν γῇ εὐθείᾳ*, Ps. 142:10 LXX) [Printed Note 15 of Violakis: The ancient Typikon, as well as the Pentekostaria, record for today this Communion Hymn and not "Take not Thy Holy Spirit from us," which Constantine [Protopsaltis] proposed and yet corrected in the Apodosis by prescribing "Thy good Spirit"]. At "With the fear of God...", we chant the Dismissal Troparion: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."; then "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and the Dismissal as indicated at Vespers [Printed Note 16 of Violakis: While the Communion Hymn is being sung, the Deacons take the time to enter the Holy Sanctuary and vest themselves; then they go forth with the Hierarchical Candle-sticks (Dikerotrikera) and stand about the throne. At the Dismissal, the Patriarch vests with Epitrachilion and Omophorion and, standing upon the throne, intones "Blessed is our God..." and begins the Introductory Psalm; the Hierarchs, having received the Antidoron, enter the Holy Sanctuary]. Paragraph 50: Great Vespers of Pentecost with the Kneeling Prayers (The Kneeling Service / Ἑσπερινὸς τῆς Γονυκλισίας): In Great Vespers, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant six verses of the three Idiomela Stichira of Matins: "Marvelous things today have all the nations seen..." (*Παράδοξα σήμερον*) (repeating each one once); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 6: "O Heavenly King, Comforter..." (*Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*). Entrance with the censer, "O Joyful Light" (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*) [Printed Note 17 of Violakis: Since a Great Prokimenon follows, "O Joyful Light" is chanted entirely outside the Sanctuary; and only at its conclusion does the Patriarch enter the Sanctuary, and from thence chants the Prokimenon]. The Great Prokimenon: Tone 7: "Who is a god so great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders!" (*Τίς Θεὸς μέγας ὡς ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν; Σὺ εἶ ὁ Θεὸς ὁ ποιῶν θαυμάσια*, Ps. 76:13–14 LXX) with its three verses, chanted once from the Sanctuary [Bema] and once by each of the two Choirs; and immediately the Great Archdeacon intones: "Again and again, bending our knees, let us pray to the Lord!" (*Ἔτι καὶ ἔτι κλίναντες τὰ γόνατα τοῦ Κυρίου δεηθῶμεν*), and while the people bend their knees, listening in reverent silence with heads uncovered, the Patriarch recites the Prayers to the hearing of all. After the second Prayer, "Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Master Almighty...", the Deacon intones: "Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up...", and the Exclamation: "For Thine it is to show mercy...". The Deacon intones: "Let us all say with our whole soul...", with the Exclamation: "For Thou art a merciful God and lovest mankind...". And immediately the Deacon intones again: "Again and again, bending our knees...", and the next two Prayers are read; at the conclusion, the Deacon intones: "Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up...", and the Exclamation: "By the favor and grace of Thine only-begotten Son...". Then "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin...". Immediately after this, the Deacon intones again: "Again and again, bending our knees...", and the last three Prayers are read [Printed Note 18 of Violakis: In the 1875 edition of the Pentekostarion, three Prayers are given for the final Stasis; but in the ancient Typikon of 1685 and in earlier editions of the Pentekostarion, these three Prayers appear united into two]; at the conclusion, the Deacon intones once again: "Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up...", and the Exclamation: "For Thou art the repose of our souls and bodies...". Then the Deacon intones: "Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord...", and the Exclamation: "For Thou art a good God and lovest mankind...". The Patriarch: "Peace be to all"... The Deacon: "Let us bow our heads unto the Lord"; the Patriarch reads the Prayer of Head-inclination and intones the Exclamation: "May the might of Thy Kingdom be blessed and glorified...".
[^483]: Kneeling Vespers Concluded (§ 50), Monday of the Holy Spirit (§§ 51–52), Fast-Free Privilege, and Apodosis of Pentecost Note. Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p847 (printed page 832) and facing English leaf p848 (printed page 833). Concluding rubrics of the Kneeling Vespers of Pentecost: At the Aposticha: the three Idiomela: Tone 3: "Now as a sign unto all men, the Spirit of the Savior hath visibly shone forth upon the Apostles..." (*Νῦν εἰς σημεῖον τοῖς πᾶσιν*); "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "Come, all ye peoples, let us worship the Godhead in three Persons..." (*Δεῦτε λαοὶ τὴν Τρισυπόστατον θεότητα*). Then "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace..." (*Νῦν ἀπολύεις*), the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion of the Feast: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..." (*Εὐλογητὸς εἶ Χριστὲ ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*) three times, and the Dismissal by the Patriarch: "May Christ our true God, Who emptied Himself, leaving the bosom of the Father, and descended from heaven upon earth...", then "Through the prayers of our Holy Fathers...". Paragraph 51: Monday of the Holy Spirit (The Feast of the Holy Spirit): After "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", we say "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!", "O Heavenly King, Comforter...", the Trisagion, Psalm 50, the Dismissal Troparion of the Feast: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...", then "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy...", and the rest of the Dismissal of the Midnight Office. Then, after the "Blessed is our God..." of Matins, the Six Psalms are recited as usual. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparion (three times), and the Psalter, the two Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis twice each, Psalm 50 recited plainly, and both Canons as yesterday without verses. After the 3rd Ode, the Kathisma: "The All-holy Spirit, proceeding from the Father, and through the Son descended upon the unlettered Disciples..." (*Τὸ Πανάγιον Πνεῦμα*) (once); after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "When the Most High came down...", the Ikos of the Feast, the Menologion, and the Hypomnema of the Monday of the Holy Spirit. The Katavasias: "Covered by a divine cloud..." (*Θείῳ καλυφθείς*) alone; then the 9th Ode is chanted. The first Exaposteilarion (twice) and the second (once). At the Praises: the three Idiomela: "By the Prophets Thou didst proclaim unto us the way of salvation..." (*Ἐν τοῖς Προφήταις ἀνήγγειλας*) to four [repeating the first] {Note: The Pentekostarion indicates that each of the three Idiomela is repeated once for a total of six}; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 8: "Once the tongues were confused on account of the audacity of the tower-building..." (*Γλῶσσαί ποτε συνεχύθησαν*). The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal Troparion: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...". Paragraph 52: Divine Liturgy of the Monday of the Holy Spirit: The Antiphons and the Entrance Hymn of the Feast as yesterday; the Dismissal Troparion and the Kontakion. Instead of the Trisagion Hymn: "As many as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia!" (*Ὅσοι εἰς Χριστὸν ἐβαπτίσθητε*). Epistle (Ephesians 5:8–19) and Gospel (Matthew 18:10–20) of the day. At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: "Rejoice, O Queen, boasting of virgins and mothers!..." (*Χαίροις Ἄνασσα*); Communion Hymn: "Take not Thy Holy Spirit from us, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind. Alleluia!" (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἅγιον μὴ ἀντανέλῃς ἀφ᾽ ἡμῶν δεόμεθα, φιλάνθρωπε*); "We have seen the true Light...", "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages", and Dismissal: "May He Who sent down the All-holy Spirit from heaven in the form of fiery tongues upon His holy Disciples and Apostles, Christ our true God...". Fast-Free Privilege: During this entire week of Holy Pentecost, fast-free privilege is observed; all foods are permitted. Apodosis of Pentecost Note: On Saturday of this week the Apodosis of the Feast of Pentecost takes place. The Service is sung exactly as prescribed in Paragraphs 47–49, with the exception, in Vespers, [Printed Note 19 of Violakis: At "Lord, I have cried", we set for the Apodosis eight verses only] of the Old Testament Readings; in Matins, of the Liti, of the Polyeleos with the Kathisma of the Third Stasis, and of the Matins Gospel. In the Divine Liturgy, we read the Epistle and Gospel of the day.
[^484]: Apodosis of Pentecost Liturgy Concluded (§ 52), Katavasias After Feast, and Sunday of All Saints: Vespers, Matins, and Liturgy (§§ 53–55). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p849 (printed page 834) and facing English leaf p850 (printed page 835). Concluding rubrics of the Apodosis of Pentecost: At "Especially", in place of "It is truly right" [Axion Estin / Zadostoinyk]: the Katavasia: "Rejoice, O Queen, boasting of virgins and mothers!..." (*Χαίροις Ἄνασσα*); Communion Hymn: "Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the land of uprightness. Alleluia!" (*Τὸ Πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἀγαθόν*); then "We have seen the true Light...", "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages", the Dismissal, and the Apodosis. Rubric for Katavasias After the Apodosis: After the Apodosis of the Feast, the following Katavasias are sung henceforth: "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..." (*Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*); and in the Divine Liturgy, we sing the Kontakion: "O Protection of Christians unashamed, Mediatress before the Creator unwavering..." (*Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν*). Paragraph 53: The First Sunday After Pentecost: All Saints: Great Vespers: On Saturday evening, following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Psalter (First Kathisma: "Blessed is the man", Pss. 1–3 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant six Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 8 and four of the Saints; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "The divine choir of the Martyrs, the foundation of the Church, the perfection of the Gospel..." (*Μαρτύρων θεῖος χορός*); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 6: "Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?..." (*Τίς μὴ μακαρίσει σε* / *Ὁ βασιλεὺς τῶν οὐρανῶν*). Entrance with censer, "O Joyful Light" (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), the Prokimenon of the day: "The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty!" (*Ὁ Κύριος ἐβασίλευσεν*, Ps. 92:1 LXX), and the Old Testament Readings (Isaiah 43:9–14; Wisdom of Solomon 3:1–9; 5:15–6:3 LXX). At the Aposticha: the Resurrectional Stichera; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "Come, all ye faithful today, let us celebrate in chorus with psalms and hymns..." (*Δεῦτε πιστοὶ σήμερον*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: "My Maker and Redeemer, Christ the Lord, proceeding from thy womb, O all-pure Virgin..." (*Ὁ Ποιητὴς καὶ λυτρωτής μου*). The Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8, that of the Saints in Tone 4: "Clothed in the blood of Thy Martyrs throughout all the world as in purple and fine linen, Thy Church through them crieth unto Thee, O Christ God: Send down Thy compassions upon Thy people; grant peace unto Thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy!" (*Τῶν ἐν ὅλῳ τῷ κόσμῳ Μαρτύρων σου*); the Theotokion, and Dismissal. Paragraph 54: Sunday Matins of All Saints: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon, the Liti of the Saints, and "It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*); then the Trisagion, the Dismissal Troparion of the Saints: "Clothed in the blood of Thy Martyrs...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparia as in Vespers; then the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX / 17th Kathisma). The Resurrectional Kathismata of the First and Second Stasis [Stichologia], the Resurrectional Evlogitaria ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), the Ypakoe of Tone 8, the Anavathmoi of Tone 8, and the Prokimenon of Tone 8. The Canons: the Resurrectional Canon and that of the Saints. After the 3rd Ode, the Resurrectional Kontakion with its Ikos recited plainly, then the Kathisma of the Saints with its Theotokion; after the 6th Ode, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "Unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation, the world offereth the God-bearing Martyrs as first-fruits of nature..." (*Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύσεως*), the Ikos of the Pentecostarion, the Menologion of the day, and the Hypomnema of the Sunday of All Saints. The Katavasias: "I shall open my mouth..." (*Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου*), and the entire order of the Matins Gospel (First Eothinon: Matthew 28:16–20); then we chant with verses: "More honorable than the Cherubim..." (*Τὴν τιμιωτέραν τῶν Χερουβίμ*). Exaposteilaria: the Resurrectional Exaposteilarion, the two of the Saints, and the Theotokion. At the Praises: four Resurrectional Stichera and four of the Saints; "Glory...": First Eothinon Doxastikon in Tone 1: "When the Disciples were hastening to the mountain for the Ascension on high..." (*Εἰς τὸ Ὄρος τοῖς Μαθηταῖς*); "Both now and ever...": Tone 2: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..." (*Ὑπερευλογημένη ὑπάρχεις*). The Great Doxology, and "Today is salvation come into the world..." (*Σήμερον σωτηρία*). Paragraph 55: Divine Liturgy of All Saints: The Typika and the four Beatitudes of Tone 8, and four hymns from the 6th Ode of the Canon of the Saints: "As an honorable, chosen living temple..." (*Ὡς ἔντιμον, ἐκλεκτόν*). After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, that of the Saints, that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion: "Unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation...". The Epistle of the Saints (Hebrews 11:33–12:2) and Gospel of the Saints (Matthew 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30). At "Especially", "It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*).
[^485]: All Saints Divine Liturgy Concluded (§ 55). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p851 (printed page 836) and facing English leaf p852 (printed page 837). Concluding rubrics of the Divine Liturgy of the Sunday of All Saints: After the Little Entrance, the Resurrectional Dismissal Troparion, that of the Saints in Tone 4 ("Clothed in the blood of Thy Martyrs throughout all the world as in purple and fine linen, Thy Church through them crieth unto Thee, O Christ God: Send down Thy compassions upon Thy people; grant peace unto Thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy!"), that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion in Tone 8: "Unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation, the world offereth the God-bearing Martyrs as first-fruits of nature; by their supplications preserve Thy Church and Thy commonwealth in profound peace, through the Theotokos, O Thou Who art greatly merciful!" (*Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύσεως*). The Epistle of the Saints (Hebrews 11:33–12:2) and Gospel of the Saints (Matthew 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30). At "Especially", "It is truly right to bless thee, O Theotokos..." (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*); Communion Hymn: "Rejoice in the Lord, O you righteous, praise is meet for the upright. Alleluia!" (*Ἀγαλλιᾶσθε δίκαιοι ἐν Κυρίῳ, τοῖς εὐθέσι πρέπει αἴνεσις*, Ps. 32:1 LXX); "We have seen the true Light..." (*Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*), "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth, and unto the ages" (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and Dismissal. This marks the conclusion of Part II of the Typikon (The Triodion and Pentekostarion Cycles).
